# 2026-07-30 (twenty-third round, a literature round not a framework round): the rank-coupled
# exponential sum already has a published rigorous bound (Tao, Prop. 1.17) and new published
# numerics (Toguchi 2026). Both were reachable only by full-text search. Net result: square-root
# cancellation for S(t) is REFUTED, and the whole S(t) program is capped at j*(l) <= 1.585*l.

Task: an extended literature search for a technique bounding `S(t)`, deliberately using terms more
distant than the 21 arXiv queries already logged today, and sources beyond arXiv. Two new
rate-limited clients were written (`experiments/E-004-arxiv-search-loop/openalex_crossref_query.py`
for OpenAlex + CrossRef polite pool, `s2_query.py` for Semantic Scholar) alongside the existing
`arxiv_query.py`, all logging to their own jsonl.

## The methodological point that unlocked this round

**The arXiv API indexes metadata only, never full text.** Every prior H-003 search round used it.
A phrase that appears *inside* a paper but not in its title or abstract is invisible to
`http://export.arxiv.org/api/query`. Confirmed directly: `all:"Syracuse random variable"` returns
**zero** hits, while OpenAlex's `filter=fulltext.search:"Syracuse random variable"` returns Tao's
own paper plus four others. Twenty-two rounds of "no literature found on rank-coupled exponential
sums" were, in part, an artifact of the search tool. Semantic Scholar's unauthenticated tier
returns immediate 429s and never delivered a result despite full backoff; OpenAlex and CrossRef
worked without a single rate-limit hit.

## Finding 1: Tao's Syracuse random variable is the same rank-coupled object, verified exactly

`Syrac(Z/3^n Z) = sum_{m=1}^{n} 3^{m-1} 2^{-a_[1,m]}`, `a_j` iid `Geom(2)` (Tao, Forum Math. Pi 10
(2022) e12, Definition 1.7 / eq. 1.21). The project already knew the object identity qualitatively
(H-002, L-003). What is made exact here: both `R_{l-1,j} mod 3^l` and `Syrac` are unit multiples of
one common core sum

    G(a_2,...,a_l) = sum_{i=1}^{l} 3^{i-1} 2^{-(a_2+...+a_i)},   a_j >= 1,

specifically `our value = 2^{s_l + B} * G` with `B = sum_{m=2}^{l} a_m`, and `Syrac = 2^{-a_1} * G`.
**The two objects differ only in the measure on gap tuples**: uniform-with-a-budget-cap
(`s_l + B <= l+j-1`) for ours, product-geometric-uncapped for Tao's.

Verified by exact set equality in `verify_syrac_fourier.py` part (B), for `(l,j)` =
(2,2), (2,3), (3,2), (3,3), (3,4), (4,3), (4,5), including this project's own ground-truth
checkpoint `R_{1,2} mod 9 = {1,2,5,7}`. Not asserted from staring at definitions.

## Finding 2: a rigorous bound on this exact sum exists in the literature, uncited here for 22 rounds

**Tao, Proposition 1.17**: for `xi` in `Z/3^n Z` with `3` not dividing `xi`, and any fixed `A > 0`,

    | E exp(-2 pi i xi Syrac(Z/3^n Z) / 3^n) |  <<_A  n^{-A}

(super-polynomial decay), proved via the Lemma 1.12 renormalization
`Syrac_{n+1} = 2^{-a}(3 Syrac_n + 1)`, which is precisely a device for converting rank-coupling into
recursion depth. `grep` over the whole repository confirms "1.17" appears nowhere: 22 rounds spent
looking for "a technique to bound S(t)" while the one published nontrivial bound on essentially that
sum sat in a paper already indexed as L-006 and marked "read in full". Round 17 reached the correct
*qualitative* verdict ("Tao's machinery gives distributional/renewal-scale control, not pointwise"),
but never identified the specific proposition or quantified the gap.

Honest caveat: Lemma 1.12's recursion is, structurally, the same renormalization as round 6's
3-adic transfer operator. Finding 2 is therefore best described as identifying the *strongest
published quantitative output* of a framework this project had already reached qualitatively, not as
a new framework.

## Finding 3: an exact convex-combination bridge from Tao's psi_n to this project's S(t)

Write `psi_n(xi) = E exp(-2 pi i xi Syrac/3^n)`, group gap tuples by total budget `B`, and set
`L_{B,u}(xi) = sum over gap tuples with total B of exp(-2 pi i xi 2^u G / 3^n)`. Then

    psi_n(xi) = sum_{B,u} w_{B,u} * ( L_{B,u}(xi) / |T_B| ),    |T_B| = C(B-1, n-2),
    w_{B,u} = 2^{-B-u} |T_B|,   sum of weights = 1 exactly.

The `L_{B,u}` are, up to a unit twist of the frequency, exactly this project's fixed-budget `S(t)`.
Verified numerically at the extremal frequency for `n = 3,4,5`: rebuilt value matches `psi_n` to
`< 1e-8` and the weights sum to `1.0000000000`. This is the bridge that makes published numerics on
`psi_n` say something rigorous about *our* `S(t)`.

## Finding 4: new published numerics on exactly this sum (L-063), independently reproduced

T. Toguchi, "Numerical observations on extremal Fourier coefficients of the Syracuse random
variable", Zenodo, doi 10.5281/zenodo.20490181, dated 18 May 2026, independent researcher (Tokyo).
Computes `M_n := max_k |psi_n(2^k)|` over the full domain for `n <= 21` (`3^21 ~ 1.05e10`; `n=20,21`
on a 961 GB cloud instance), two independent implementations, agreeing to `<1e-10` for `n <= 8`.

**Independently reproduced here before being trusted**, by two methods of my own (forward
construction of the exact distribution via Lemma 1.12 then DFT; and the dual-space recursion):
`M_n` AND the argmax `k*(n)` match the paper exactly for **n = 1..9** (`verify_syrac_fourier.py`
part (A), all nine rows `YES`). This preprint is numerically sound in the range checkable here,
unlike several other independent-researcher preprints already triaged in this project's index.

Reported values: `M_9 = 4.80262e-2`, `M_15 = 1.62845e-2`, `M_21 = 7.87213e-3`; a power-law fit
`M_n ~ C n^{-2.1}` over `9 <= n <= 21`; an exponential fit is ~20x worse in residual. The argmax
location drifts with slope near `4/3` and `k*(21)/21 = 4/3` exactly. Tao's own Remark 1.15
conjectures the bound should be improvable to `O(exp(-c n))` for some absolute `c > 0`.

## Finding 5 (the real result of this round): square-root cancellation for S(t) is REFUTED

If every layer sum obeyed square-root cancellation, `|L_B| <= sqrt(|T_B|)`, then by the *exact*
convex combination of Finding 3,

    M_n  <=  Q(n) := sum_B 2^{-B} sqrt( C(B-1, n-2) ).

`Q(n)` is computed exactly (no asymptotics, exact binomials). Measured against the reproduced `M_n`:

```
  n   M_n measured   Q(n) ceiling   M_n/Q(n)   verdict
  7    7.58700e-02    1.00287e-01      0.76    consistent
  8    6.08907e-02    6.04975e-02      1.01    REFUTED
  9    4.80262e-02    3.62866e-02      1.32    REFUTED
 10    3.82783e-02    2.16675e-02      1.77    REFUTED
 15    1.62845e-02    1.57521e-03     10.34    REFUTED
 21    7.87213e-03    6.43633e-05    122.31    REFUTED
```

**The refutation already bites at `n = 8, 9`, inside the range independently recomputed here, so it
does not depend on trusting Toguchi's large-`n` data at all.** The violation factor grows roughly
geometrically (about `1.45` per unit `n` from `n=10` to `n=21`), so no constant and no polylog
correction rescues it either: a hypothetical `|L_B| <= C (log)^k sqrt(|T_B|)` would need `C` growing
exponentially in `n`.

This kills, rigorously, the most optimistic scenario every Fourier-flavoured round was implicitly
hoping for. The distribution of this rank-coupled sum is only *polynomially* close to uniform, which
is consistent with Tao proving `n^{-A}` rather than anything exponential, and with his own
Remark 1.15 conjecturing at best `exp(-cn)`.

## Finding 6 (strategic, and the reason this round matters): the entire S(t) program is capped

The `L^1` (triangle-inequality / major-arc) covering criterion, which every prior round's framework
reduces to (round 5 states it explicitly: bound `sum_{xi != 0}|S(xi)|` small enough to force
`N(r) > 0`), needs `sum_{xi != 0} |S(xi)| < T` with `T = |index set|`. With about `3^l` frequencies
that needs the normalized extremal coefficient below about `3^{-l}`. Even **perfect** square-root
cancellation `|S| ~ sqrt(T)` therefore only yields `T >~ 3^{2l}`, i.e. `4^j >~ 3^{2l}`, i.e.

    j  >=  2 log_4(3) * l  =  1.5849625... * l.

Set that beside what is already known:

```
  conjectured truth                       log_4(3) * l  =  0.7925 l
  ceiling of the whole L^1 Fourier route  2 log_4(3) * l = 1.5850 l
  already proven, elementarily (round 21) (5/3) l        = 1.6667 l
```

So the 22-round program of bounding `S(t)`, **even executed perfectly to the square-root barrier
that Finding 5 just showed is false anyway**, could at best have improved the leading constant from
`1.6667` to `1.5850`, and provably cannot reach the `log_4(3) * l` scale at which `e(l)` lives.
No amount of progress on `S(t)` via this route can distinguish `log l` from `sqrt l` from linear
growth of `e(l)`.

Labeled per Rule 10b: Findings 1, 3, 4 are verified computations; Finding 5 is a refutation
conditional only on numerics reproduced here at `n <= 9`; Finding 6 is an elementary barrier
statement about a *method*, not a theorem about `j*(l)`.

## Search coverage this round (negative results, recorded so they are not repeated)

arXiv (24 further queries, logged in `arxiv_search_log.jsonl`), all zero or irrelevant:
multilinear oscillatory integrals (Christ-Li-Tao-Thiele: real literature, but its coupling lives in
the *phase*, whereas ours lives in the *ordering constraint on the domain*, so the framework does
not apply), Bramson/minimal-displacement and consistent-minimal-displacement BRW, coupon-collector
second-order thresholds, lossy-source-coding dispersion and second-order coding rates, covering-code
asymptotic redundancy, Bernoulli convolutions / Garsia entropy / self-similar-measure Fourier decay,
shift radix systems and canonical number systems, sofic-shift anticipation, worst-case universal-code
redundancy, large deviations for mean-payoff games, Lyndon words, record processes, prefix codes with
rank-dependent cost, additive bases in powers of 2, digit expansions with variable weight.

OpenAlex: full-text and citation-graph passes. The 141 works citing Tao's paper were scanned; nothing
bearing on `S(t)` beyond what is above (mostly Collatz-adjacent surveys, claimed proofs, and Siegel's
non-archimedean program already indexed as L-013).

## Where this leaves H-003

The recommendation from rounds 6, 17 and 18 ("stop opening new framework rounds against `S(t)`") is
now backed by a quantitative reason rather than an accumulation of failures: the target was capped at
`1.585 l` all along. Any future work on `e(l)`'s growth rate should be aimed at the extreme-value /
worst-residue structure directly (the `max_z min-cost` object of rounds 19-21), not at the Fourier
transform of the whole family.

## Same-day refinements and corrections to this round (recorded per Rule 8c)

### Correction 1, from the Codex consult: the `n^{-2.1}` fit is not an asymptotic law

Codex's first substantive point: Tao's Proposition 1.17 is uniform over frequencies not divisible by
`3`, and powers of `2` run through exactly those frequencies, so `M_n` must eventually decay faster
than **every** fixed power of `n`. The measured `n^{-2.1}` therefore cannot be an asymptotic law.

Checked, and correct. Toguchi says as much himself (his Remark 4.4 and Remark 4.6 both flag the fit
as finite-range only, compatible with an effective exponent that increases with `n`). Finding 5 is
unaffected because it is a **finite-`n`** statement: for each `n` in `8..21`, the measured `M_n`
exceeds the exact ceiling `Q(n)`. Nothing in it needs `M_n ~ n^{-2.1}` to persist.

What the correction does invalidate is the extrapolation sentence written above ("the violation
factor grows roughly geometrically ... so no constant and no polylog correction rescues it either").
That is an empirical extrapolation past `n=21`, not a theorem, and it is withdrawn as stated.
The defensible version, sharpened below, is that whether the violation persists asymptotically turns
on a single explicit threshold.

### Refinement: the square-root ceiling is exactly `3^{-n/2}`, with a saddle at budget ratio `4/3`

`Q(n) = sum_B 2^{-B} sqrt(C(B-1,n-2))`. Writing `B = beta*n` and maximizing
`f(beta) = (1/2)[beta ln beta - (beta-1) ln(beta-1)] - beta ln 2` gives
`f'(beta) = (1/2) ln(beta/(beta-1)) - ln 2 = 0`, i.e. `beta/(beta-1) = 4`, so

    beta* = 4/3   exactly,   and   max f = f(4/3) = -(ln 3)/2   exactly.

So `Q(n) ~ 3^{-n/2}`, confirmed numerically (`-ln Q(n)/n` = 0.4596, 0.4832, 0.5069, 0.5213, 0.5298,
0.5369 at `n` = 21, 30, 50, 80, 120, 200, climbing to `(ln 3)/2 = 0.549306`). This is exactly the
"square-root cancellation relative to the group size `3^n`" scale, as it has to be.

The three scales that matter now line up with exact constants:

```
  what the L^1 covering criterion needs      |nu-hat|  <   3^{-n}
  best conceivable (square-root cancellation) |nu-hat| ~   3^{-n/2}
  measured reality (M_n, n <= 21)             |nu-hat|  >   3^{-n/2}
```

and the factor of `2` between the first two lines is precisely the factor of `2` in Finding 6's
`2 log_4(3) = 1.5850` versus `log_4(3) = 0.7925`. One coherent picture, not three coincidences.

Sharpened asymptotic statement, replacing the withdrawn extrapolation: square-root cancellation for
these layer sums is compatible with Tao's conjectured `M_n = O(exp(-c n))` (his Remark 1.15) **only
if `c >= (ln 3)/2 = 0.5493`**, and the `L^1` covering route needs `c > ln 3 = 1.0986`. Tao states no
value of `c`. So even the optimistic conjecture, at an unspecified `c`, may or may not clear the
square-root bar, and clears the covering bar only at twice that rate.

### One numerical coincidence, flagged and explicitly not claimed

The saddle budget ratio derived above is `beta* = B/n = 4/3`, and Toguchi's measured extremal
**frequency** drift is `k*(n)/n -> 4/3` (exactly `4/3` at `n=21`). These are a priori different
quantities (one a total-gap-budget ratio, one a discrete logarithm base 2 of the extremal frequency),
and both arise from saddle-point structure in the same sum, so a common cause is plausible. Recorded
as an observation to check, not as a result. Also worth noting `4/3` sits inside the observed
`j*(l)/l` range (1.333 at `l=15` down to 1.174 at `l=23`) and inside the `rho_k` sequence; that could
easily be numerology and is not offered as evidence of anything.

## Finding 7: the redirect, made concrete from this project's own already-verified data

Finding 6 says stop bounding `S(t)`. The natural replacement is already built: round 21's
mean-payoff-game certificates. Each `k` yields a verified potential certificate proving
`j*(l) <= rho_k * l + C_k` for **every** `l`, with `C_k = max h - min h`. Subtracting `l log_4(3)`:

    e(l)  <=  min_k [ (rho_k - log_4(3)) * l  +  C_k ]        (exact, for every l)

This is rigorous, given round 21's certificates (verified there four independent ways), and it is
consistent with ground truth (at `l=23` the bound is 22.27 against the actual `e(23) = 8.773`).

**Correction, 2026-07-30 (full critique round, see the round-24 "critique" section below): the
optimization exponent below was computed wrong, and the wrong version was repeated three times
(here, in the round-23 addendum below, and in `HYPOTHESES.md`'s H-009 row) before being caught.**
Minimizing `g(k) = A*l*k^{-p} + B*k` over `k` gives `k* = (p*A*l/B)^{1/(p+1)}`, and BOTH terms of
`g(k*)` scale as `l^{1/(p+1)}`, not `l^{p/(p+1)}`. (Independently re-verified twice: by hand via
the stationary-point calculation, and numerically via `scipy.optimize.minimize_scalar` at three
values of `l` for `p=1, 0.84, 0.5`, matching `1/(p+1)` to 3+ significant figures each time and
decisively ruling out `p/(p+1)`.) The two formulas coincide only at `p=1` (both give `1/2`), which
is exactly why the error went unnoticed: the one value anyone actually checked by eye (the `p=1`
sqrt case) is the one value where the bug is invisible.

The corrected asymptotic dictionary, if `rho_k - log_4(3) ~ A/k^p` and `C_k ~ B*k`:

```
  p = 1              ->  e(l) = O(sqrt l)          (the sqrt branch of E-002's trichotomy)
  p < 1              ->  e(l) = O(l^{1/(p+1)}),  exponent > 1/2  (WORSE than sqrt, not better)
  p > 1              ->  e(l) = O(l^{1/(p+1)}),  exponent < 1/2
  rho_k -> L > log_4(3) ->  the bound stays linear, and the linear branch survives
```

This reverses Finding 7's original reading of its own data. On the existing data `C_k` is close to
linear in `k`, and `(rho_k - log_4(3))*k` is increasing (meaning `p < 1` on the accessible range,
now confirmed through `k=13`: fitting `p` from the `k=3` and `k=13` gap values gives `p ~ 0.84`,
from `k=6` and `k=13` gives `p ~ 0.90`), which under the CORRECTED formula gives an exponent of
`1/(1+p) ~ 0.52-0.54` -- at or slightly WORSE than `sqrt(l)`, not better. The originally-stated
conclusion ("exponent below 1/2") was backwards. Eleven points over sticky rational values still
cannot settle the true asymptotic `p`, and the qualitative fallback (any `theta<1` still refutes
the linear branch, and `rho_k -> L > log_4(3)` still keeps the linear branch alive) is unaffected
by this correction. What changes is the specific numeric reading of the current data: it no longer
suggests a sub-sqrt exponent is likely; if anything, at face value it suggests the opposite.

**This converts H-003's open question into a computation on finite, exactly-solvable games with no
Fourier analysis anywhere: extend `rho_k` and `C_k` to larger `k` and determine the joint
asymptotics.** An upper bound of the form `e(l) = O(l^{theta})` with `theta < 1` would already refute
the currently-live linear branch, which would be a real result. Logged as its own hypothesis per
Rule 8e. Note the state space is `3^k`, so `k=14` is about 4.8M states; this must wait for the
machine's memory to free up (at the time of writing, 700 MB free with the `l=24` run and `mpg4 12 13`
both resident), and the `C_k` extraction, not just `rho_k`, has to be recorded this time.

Literature check on the framing: the object is the growth of the relative value function (bias /
corrector) of an average-cost control problem on a non-compact state space. Twelve targeted arXiv
queries (average-cost MDPs with unbounded costs, vanishing discount, turnpike rates, Poisson
equation with unbounded solution, correctors and convergence rates in stochastic homogenization,
max-plus eigenvectors, min-plus covering radius) returned real literature but nothing transferable:
the homogenization rate results closest in spirit (e.g. arXiv:1206.2601, Hamilton-Jacobi
homogenization error estimates) are continuum results for random media, not a deterministic 3-adic
game. No technique import; the direction is computational.

## Status of the independent check (honest, per Rule 11/8c)

The Codex consult (`gpt-5.6-sol`, `model_reasoning_effort=max`, full context, launched in the
background) delivered the one substantive correction recorded above and then stalled: its transcript
shows the bubblewrap/user-namespace sandbox warning this project has been burned by before, followed
by roughly a dozen `web search:` lines with empty queries and no returned results, and no verdict on
questions 1-4. **Findings 5b and 6 therefore have NOT been independently confirmed by a second
model.** They passed my own adversarial scrutiny and every arithmetic step is reproduced in
`verify_syrac_fourier.py`, but the strategic claim that redirects the program deserves a genuinely
independent read (a different vendor, or the researcher) before anything is built on it. Flagged, not
papered over.

## A real gap found in this project's own literature index

A CrossRef pass turned up three Wirsching items predating the 1998 book, none of them in
`literature/INDEX.md`:

- "An improved estimate concerning 3n+1 predecessor sets", Acta Arith. 63 (1993) 205-210,
  doi:10.4064/aa-63-3-205-210
- "On the combinatorial structure of 3N+1 predecessor sets", Discrete Math. (1996),
  doi:10.1016/0012-365X(94)00243-C
- "3n+1 predecessor densities and uniform distribution in Z*_3" (1996, OpenAlex W2342110413)

The third is, by title, the closest published work to this project's exact question that has ever
turned up. The index's standing summary ("no paper engages the covering conjecture past 1998/2003")
was about *successors*; these are the *precursors*, and the 1998 book's Chapter V grew out of them.
Whether they contain a sharper quantitative statement about the distribution of `R_{j,k}` mod `3^l`
is unknown and unchecked. Added to the index and logged as a hypothesis to read them.

## Primary-source verification of Finding 2 (Rule 11: Toguchi's transcription is not a source)

Fetched `arXiv:1909.03562v3` directly and read the relevant statements in the PDF text.

**Proposition 1.17 confirmed verbatim** ("Decay of characteristic function"): "Let `n >= 1`, and let
`xi in Z/3^n Z` be not divisible by 3. Then `|E e^{-2 pi i xi Syrac(Z/3^n Z)/3^n}| <<_A n^{-A}` for
any fixed `A > 0`." Toguchi's transcription is accurate.

**Lemma 1.12 confirmed to exist, with one difference worth recording.** Tao's Lemma 1.12 is the
pointwise probability recursion, with a restricted sum over `1 <= a <= 2*3^n` subject to
`2^a x = 1 mod 3` and a normalizing denominator `1 - 2^{-2*3^n}`. Toguchi works with the untruncated
infinite-sum form, whose geometric tail normalizes to 1 directly. The two differ by
`O(2^{-2*3^n})`, astronomically below double precision, and my two independent reproductions of his
table agree with it exactly, so this is a presentational difference, not an error.

**The `exp(-cn)` conjecture confirmed, with a citation correction.** Toguchi attributes it to
"Remark 1.15". Tao's Remark 1.15 does predict `exp(-cm)`, but for Proposition 1.14; the same
speculation for Proposition 1.17 specifically sits in an unnumbered paragraph just after it: "it is
possible that the bound in Proposition 1.17 could be improved, perhaps to as far as `O(exp(-cn))`
for some `c > 0`. However, we will not need or pursue such a bound here." Cite the paragraph, not
Remark 1.15, if this is ever written up.

**Tao states this project's rank-coupling obstruction himself, in print.** Immediately after
introducing (1.24), he writes: "If the Syracuse random variable ... *was the sum of independent
random variables, then the characteristic function of `Syrac(Z/3^n Z)` would factor as something
like a Riesz product of cosines, and its estimation would be straightforward. Unfortunately, the
expression (1.24) does not obviously resolve into such a sum of independent random variables*", and
"The remaining task is to establish Proposition 1.17. This turns out to be the most difficult step
in the argument, and is carried out in Section 7."

That is precisely the obstruction this project has been calling rank-coupling: the rank-dependent
`3^{m-1}` weight sits on a partial sum `a_{[1,m]}`, so the phase does not factor over independent
coordinates. **Section 7 of Tao's paper is therefore a published technique for partially overcoming
exactly this obstruction**, and the conclusion recorded across 22 rounds ("no literature found on
rank/position-weighted exponential sums") was wrong: the most relevant treatment in existence is a
section of a paper already sitting in this index as L-006, marked read in full. The reason it was
missed is Rule-13-relevant rather than mathematical: the arXiv API cannot see inside a paper, and
every prior search round used only the arXiv API.

Also confirmed: Tao's Remark 1.15 runs the same entropy-crossing heuristic this project uses
(`Geom(2)` has Shannon entropy `log 4`, so `Geom(2)^n` behaves like a uniform distribution on
`4^{n+o(n)}` tuples, against a range of cardinality `3^n`). Independent corroboration of the
`log_4(3)` threshold's provenance, from the primary source.

Honest bottom line on Finding 2, unchanged by this verification: Section 7 is the right citation and
the right technique, and it is still quantitatively far short of what H-003 needs, because `n^{-A}`
is not `3^{-n}` (Finding 6).

### Addendum, same day: `rho_12 = 7/6` from a concurrent session

A concurrent session committed `rho_12 = 7/6` (commit `0b13f73`) while this round was being written,
with a `k=13` sweep still running. Folded into Finding 7's diagnostic: `(rho_k - log_4(3)) * k` now
reads 4.12, 4.30, 4.48, 4.49 for `k = 9, 10, 11, 12`, still increasing, so `p < 1` still holds on
the accessible range. **Correction, 2026-07-30 (critique round)**: under the formula error fixed
in Finding 7 above, `p < 1` implies the exponent `1/(1+p)` is ABOVE `1/2`, not below it; the
sentence originally here ("the implied exponent stays below 1/2") had the same sign error and is
now corrected by reference to that fix, not repeated separately. **The running sweep should be made
to record `C_k`, not only `rho_k`** (H-009 needs both sequences; `C_k = max h - min h` from the
same potential the solver already computes, so it is free to extract and cannot be recovered later
without re-solving).

# 2026-07-30 (twenty-fourth round): a generation round targeting the three named gaps directly

Different in kind from a proof-audit round. The researcher asked, given an honest inventory of what
this project does and does not have (extended table, the `rho_k` bound sequence through `7/6`, the
elementary unboundedness proof, the Fourier-ceiling comparison from round 23, and H-002's unresolved
bridge), for `gpt-5.6-sol` (`max` reasoning, standard config) to propose three distinct, checkable
directions for each of three named gaps: (A) proof or refutation of WCC itself, (B) the exact growth
rate of `e(l)`, (C) H-002's entropy-count bridge. Full prompt (including the complete "already tried"
list, so it would not resubmit anything) and transcript at
`/home/rat/.claude/jobs/a8d2d60e/tmp/codex_round24_gaps_{prompt,transcript,lastmsg}.txt`. This
section covers GAP A and GAP B; GAP C (which targets H-002, not H-003) is recorded in
`notes/H-002.md` instead. Genuinely different from rounds 16-23 in one respect: the model ran real
web searches this time to check its own citations before answering (visible in the transcript), and
the two citations it produced both verify against primary sources (Birkhoff 1957, checked against
CrossRef; Tao's blog post `beta=1`/`c_n` framing, already independently confirmed present in L-003
by this project's own earlier read of L-006). Neither citation is fabricated.

## GAP A: proof or refutation of WCC itself

**A1, low-rank absorber (confidence 30%, self-rated).** Split a witness into an `r`-rank prefix
(`r=o(l)`) and an `(l-r)`-rank bulk, via a claimed exact concatenation identity
`G(uv) = G(u) + 3^r * 2^{-A(u)} * G(v)`. If true, concatenating any bulk-covering witness onto a
prefix that steers the required bulk target away from a (conjecturally sparse) exceptional set would
prove WCC outright, using only `O(r)=o(l)` extra budget. Not verified: the concatenation identity
itself needs checking against this project's actual `G`/value definition before anything else, and
the load-bearing step (a sparse-exceptional-set-in-every-cylinder statement, then a transversal
construction) is not obviously easier than the original problem. Worth a first pass: verify the
identity numerically (cheap, small `l`) before deciding whether to invest further.

**A2, disproof via a starved coherent cylinder (confidence 42%).** A concrete refutation strategy:
search for a nested sequence of shallow (`r=floor(theta*l)`) residues `b_r` whose cylinder occupancy
`p_{l,r}(b_r)` decays exponentially in `l`, which would force `j*(l)` above any linear rate and
refute WCC. Directly checkable with existing tools: this project's own exact tables already have
`p_{l,r}(b)` for every `(l,r,b)` computed so far (it is literally the H-002 tuple-count data, just
not yet mined this way). Flagged as the cheapest of the nine to give a genuine first look, since it
needs no new code, only a new query against data already on disk.

**A3, dual certificate strengthened to a fixed-target refutation protocol (confidence 27%).**
Proposes upgrading E-003's own mean-payoff-game certificate from "some hidden-digit adversary" to
"a single coherent `z in Z_3^*`" by requiring the potential's telescoping inequality to hold around
every action-tree diamond, not just along the optimal cycle. If the strengthened certificate fails
existing policies (`k` up to 12-13 already solved), that failure is itself informative: it would show
the current bound is a window-relaxation artifact rather than a real obstruction to disproof. Directly
buildable on `experiments/E-003-mpg-cylinder/certificate.py` with no new theory needed to attempt a
first check. The single most actionable GAP A item given what already exists in this repository.

## GAP B: the growth rate of `e(l)`

**B1, endpoint-conditioned Doob bridge for `O(sqrt(l))` (confidence 35%).** Condition the unbudgeted
geometric process on hitting the target residue `z` via a Doob `h`-transform, turning the rank
coupling into the bridge's own transition law rather than a phase to cancel. The load-bearing claim
(uniform-in-`z` mean/variance/tail control for the conditioned cost) would be attacked via projective
contraction in the Hilbert metric, citing Garrett Birkhoff, "Extensions of Jentzsch's theorem," Trans.
Amer. Math. Soc. 85 (1957), 219-227, DOI `10.1090/S0002-9947-1957-0087058-6`. **Citation verified
against CrossRef this round**: real paper, correct pages, correct journal. A legitimate, well-known
piece of positive-operator machinery; whether it actually delivers uniformity across all `z` is the
open question, not whether the tool exists.

**B2, uniform local limit theorem inside each residue fiber for `O(log(l))` (confidence 25%).**
Fixes the residue and does one-dimensional Fourier analysis only in the budget variable (fiber
polynomial `Z_{l,z}(w)`), explicitly not a repackaging of `S(t)` since the endpoint never varies. The
model's own stated risk (a target-dependent phase transition or lattice-span anomaly in the fiber
polynomials) is specific and plausible, not generic hedging.

**B3, sharpened lower bound via a completion-weighted rare cylinder (confidence 62%, the highest
self-rating in GAP A/B).** Same shallow-cylinder-occupancy object as A2, aimed instead at extending
the already-proven `(1/4)*log_2(l)` term rather than a full refutation: any sub-polynomial bound on
the least-loaded shallow cylinder's occupancy adds a matching term to the lower bound. Cites Tao's
Lemma 1.12 (already read and cross-checked by this project, L-006) as the source of candidate
least-loaded branches. Like A2, this is checkable against existing on-disk data before any new theory
is needed.

## Assessment

No item is a finished argument; the model was asked for directions, not proofs, and answered that way
honestly (self-rated confidences range 25-62%, none claimed as settled). Two things distinguish this
round from the weaker parts of rounds 16-20: both external citations check out against primary
sources (Rule 11), and two of the six items (A2, B3) are directly queryable against data this project
already has on disk, with no new computation or theory required for a first honest look. Recommended
next step, cheapest first: mine the existing H-002 tuple-count table for A2/B3's shallow-cylinder
occupancy question before writing any new code. A3 (strengthening E-003's own certificate) is the
next cheapest, reusing `certificate.py` directly. B1 and B2 are real research programs, not quick
checks; not started this round.

## Round-24 follow-up (same day): A1 checked, A2/B3 mined -- one preliminary but potentially
## significant pattern found, needs independent review before trusting further

Full scripts, README, and headline finding at `experiments/E-005-round24-checks/`. This section
records the reasoning; that folder has the reproducible code.

### A1: the concatenation identity is false as literally stated, true once corrected

Codex's formula `G(uv) = G(u) + 3^r * 2^{-A(u)} * G(v)`, with `A(u)` read as "the cost of the
prefix `u` alone," fails in every one of 5000 random trials (exact `Fraction` arithmetic, no
modular reduction, so this is the underlying algebraic identity, not a residue-class artifact).
Hand derivation before running the check found why: splitting `G`'s defining sum at rank `r`
leaves a genuine three-way structure, prefix / connecting ("join") gap / suffix, not a clean
two-way `(u,v)` split. The corrected identity, `A(u)` replaced by the cumulative cost through
the connecting gap (`u`'s own cost plus the first gap of what should be `v`), matches exactly in
all 5000 trials. Real, useful correction: any attempt at A1's absorber construction needs to
carry this extra join-gap degree of freedom explicitly (arguably a resource, one more free
parameter to steer the transversal with, not just a nuisance), not assume a clean two-part split.
Not pursued further this round; A1 itself was only 30%-self-rated and the load-bearing step (the
transversal construction) is untouched by this correction either way.

### A2/B3: shallow-cylinder occupancy, exact for l=4..23

Both A2 (hunt for an exponentially starved cylinder, a refutation strategy) and B3 (sharpen the
elementary lower bound via the same quantity) target `q_{l,r} := (2*3^(r-1)) * min_b p_{l,r}(b)`,
the worst-case occupancy of a depth-`r` residue cylinder under the exact critical-budget ensemble
(`j=j*(l)`). Realized that reduction mod `3^r` only depends on the `r` LARGEST chosen exponents
(this project's value convention weights rank `i` by `3^i`, `i=0` the largest exponent), so the
same validated rotation-DP can be rerun with modulus `n=3^r` instead of `3^l`: exact, and now
independent of `l` in memory cost, letting `l=4..23` (every exact known point) run in under 2
seconds total instead of the ~29GB/13-minute cost a full mod-`3^l` array needs at `l=18` alone.
Sanity-checked against the full-modulus DP's own aggregation before trusting the shortcut.

**Finding**: across every scaling tested (fixed `r`, `r~sqrt(l)`, fixed ratios `r/l~0.33` and
`~0.7`, and `r` up to 12 at `l=23`), `-log(q_{l,r})` grows roughly linearly in `r` and stays
close to constant once `l` is comfortably larger than `r` (e.g. at `l=23`, `r=1..12`, the
per-unit-`r` increase in `-log(q)` stays in a `0.03`-`0.3` band with no acceleration, not the
runaway growth an exponential-in-`l` starvation would produce). No sign of `q_{l,r}` decaying
exponentially in `l` at any scaling tried.

**Read on A2**: weighs against the refutation hope in the tested range (`l<=23`, `r<=12`); no
starved branch found. Does not rule out refutation at scales beyond what was tested.

**Read on B3, the more interesting one**: if `q_{l,r} ~ exp(-c*r)` holds robustly even as `r`
grows WITH `l` (`r=l^delta` for fixed `delta<1`, not just the fixed-`r`/fixed-ratio regimes
tested here), the resulting `e(l)` lower bound would be `~c'*l^delta - O(1)`, well past the
current elementary `(1/4)*log_2(l) - O(1)` bound, and past B3's own "sub-polynomial `q`" target
too (`exp(-c*l^delta)` decays faster than any polynomial in `l`). **This is not yet a result.**
The `r=l^delta`-with-`l` regime itself was not actually tested (only `l<=23` was available, so
`r` growing with `l` and `l` growing large simultaneously could not be separated from each
other); a naive attempt to push `l` past 23 by guessing `j` (either a constant offset on
`log_4(3)*l`, or the proven-safe `rho_13` bound with a small guessed constant for its unknown
`C_13`) undershoots the true `j*(l)` and breaks covering outright by `l~50-80` (`min_occ` hits
zero), which is itself a small, concrete illustration of why `e(l)` cannot be waved away with a
fixed constant. Extending this analysis past `l=23` needs a properly justified `j(l)` schedule
(l=24's computation, once it lands, would help, as would a rigorous a priori bound rather than
extrapolation), not attempted further this round.

**Flagged, per Rule 11b, for a genuine second opinion before investing further**: the pattern
above (roughly `r`-linear, `l`-independent occupancy floor) is numerically clean over the range
tested but is a purely empirical observation with no derivation yet, and its B3 interpretation
would be a substantial strengthening if it held. Worth a dedicated, larger, properly-`j(l)`-aware
follow-up computation, and worth putting in front of an independent model (or the researcher)
before treating it as more than a lead. Not promoted beyond `open-unexplored` status.

### Same-day resolution: the pattern does NOT survive a decisive, cheap test -- Gemini's skepticism confirmed

Sent both this pattern and the H-002 GAP C bridge (below) to Gemini (`gemini-3.6-flash`, max
thinking, genuinely independent vendor per Rule 11b) as an adversarial review. Full prompt and
response at `/home/rat/.claude/jobs/a8d2d60e/tmp/gemini_round24_{independent_check,response}.txt`.

Gemini gave 5% confidence the pattern reflects genuine `e(l)` asymptotics, with a specific,
checkable mechanism: reduction mod `3^r` depends only on the top `r` exponents, whose joint law
converges to a stationary limit as `l -> infinity` at fixed `r` (explaining the observed
`l`-independence as a generic, expected stabilization, not a signal about covering); the
approximately linear growth in `r` is generic local digit-mixing, not specific to `j*(l)`'s
critical value; and, decisively, `-log(q_{l,r})` MUST eventually saturate (roll over to
sub-linear growth) as `r` approaches `l`, because covering is known to hold at `r=l` with a
modest, already-bounded `e(l)`. Extrapolating the shallow-`r` linear trend out to `r=l^delta`
would assume the conclusion (a stronger `e(l)` lower bound) rather than derive it: exactly the
kind of circularity this project's own Rule 11 exists to catch. Gemini proposed a decisive,
cheap test using only already-trusted `l<=23` data: push `r` close to `l` (not just to 12) and
check whether `C(l,r) := -log(q_{l,r})/r` stays flat (pattern survives) or drops as `r/l -> 1`
(pattern is a shallow artifact).

**Ran it. It drops.** `r` pushed to 13 (kept safely small to avoid competing with H-002's
concurrent memory-heavy run) at `l=14,16,18,20,23`, reaching `r/l` up to `0.93` at `l=14`. In
every single `l` tested, `C(l,r)` rises to a peak around `r=2` (`C~0.64-0.73`) and then declines
monotonically all the way to the largest `r` tested (`C~0.25-0.29` at `r/l~0.6-0.93`), with no
exception. This is the rollover Gemini predicted, not the flat/sustained linear growth the
earlier (r<=12, mostly `r/l<0.5`) data suggested. **Correcting the earlier read**: what looked
like "roughly linear, `l`-independent" growth was the front end of a concave (sub-linear,
decelerating) curve, visible only because the earlier test never pushed `r` far enough relative
to `l` to see the decline. Consistent with, not evidence against, the mainstream expectation that
`e(l)` grows slowly (bounded/log-type) rather than at a polynomial-in-`l` exponent. B3's
"sub-polynomial `q`" hope is not supported by this data either, in either direction; this specific
numerical probe is now closed, not promoted further. Script and updated README at
`experiments/E-005-round24-checks/` (`rollover_test` section).

Methodological note for future rounds: an numerical pattern found by testing only a bounded range
of a two-parameter family (here, `r` up to a fixed cap regardless of `l`) can look like a genuine
trend simply because the range tested never reaches the region where a known boundary condition
must kick in. Worth checking "does the trend survive pushing the free parameter as close to its
known boundary as the exact data allows" before treating any such pattern as a lead, not just
"does the trend look clean over the range tried."

### A3: investigated in full by an Opus subagent -- not hard, provably vacuous (in the regime tested)

Full scratch code and derivation preserved by the subagent at
`/tmp/claude-1000/-home-rat-weak-covering-conjecture/a8d2d60e-9133-436f-a13c-a0fb27ee7018/scratchpad/a3/`
(not in this repo; copy out if this needs revisiting). Independently spot-checked the two most
load-bearing numerical/algebraic claims by hand before recording any of this (Rule 8c): the `-2`
fixed point (`T_0(-2) = -2` exactly, `d=0` legal since `-2 == 1 mod 3`) and the lifting-the-
exponent valuation `v_3(4^(3^k)-1) = k+1` both confirmed exactly for `k=1..7`; the "diamond"
property (three actions `d, d+2*3^k, d+4*3^k` at any unit `z` share one window mod `3^k` and
reveal all three hidden digits) confirmed on 333/333 random trials (the subagent's own count was
406/406; a bug in this session's first attempt at the same check, an uncounted skip on
`z % 3 == 0`, was found and fixed before trusting the corrected 333/333).

**Result: A3's proposed strengthening is unsatisfiable, not merely unverified.** The existing
certificate (`certificate.py`, `mpg4.adversary_lower_bound`) already has the "for every legal `d`,
some adversary digit `e`" structure A3 asked for; the only new content in Codex's proposal is
requiring all the realized hidden digits along a fixed target `z`'s own action tree to be mutually
consistent (Codex's "diamond compatibility"). The diamond property above shows this can never hold
for a window-only (not depth- or target-dependent) potential: at any window, THREE legal actions
lead to the SAME window one level down while revealing all three possible next digits, so a single
`z`'s own orbit generically realizes every digit at every window it revisits (confirmed for actual
targets `z=1,-2,5,7,11,13`: 54/54 to 485/486 of all reachable window-digit pairs realized within a
modest cost budget). Because of this, the specific 3-adic fixed point `z=-2` (`T_0(-2)=-2`, a
genuine zero-cost infinite loop) sits inside the same window-level constraint graph any A3-style
certificate must satisfy, forcing the certified rate `lambda <= 0` -- proven rigorously for window
sizes `k = O(log_3 l)` (the regime where the cheapest closing cycle, cost `2*3^k`, fits inside a
budget of order `l`), and confirmed empirically (exact min-mean-cycle computation, `k=3..7`) for
budgets far below that, well inside Codex's originally intended `k=o(l)` regime.

**Honest side result, not the original goal but worth keeping** (Rule 8e): building the lift of
each window-optimal cycle back to an actual 3-adic integer (its cost checked directly against
`step_a_grounding.py`'s exact DP, zero mismatches for `l<=7`) shows that target costs noticeably
*less* than `rho_k * l` at every `k=3,5,7` tested (e.g. `k=7`, `l=16`: lifted-target cost `15` vs
`rho_7*16=22.4` vs the true `j*(16)=20`). `rho_k` is sustained by the adversary's freedom to punish
per-action deviations across *different* hypothetical targets, not by any single genuine target
actually needing that much. Mild evidence (not proof) that the window relaxation carries a real
gap, i.e. that `inf_k rho_k` may sit strictly above `limsup_l j*(l)/l` -- directly motivating the
correction recorded above this section.

**What was not ruled out**, stated plainly per the subagent's own honest scoping: depth-dependent
potentials escape the cycle argument but reduce to restating "this `z` is hard" rather than giving
a finite certificate; potentials on a richer finite state (window plus bounded history) were not
checked and would need unboundedly much information to track a specific digit, likely collapsing
the same way but not verified; only `k<=7` and a handful of targets were tested.

A3 closed as a lead: the mechanism Codex proposed does not work, established rather than merely
suspected. The side result on `rho_k`'s possible looseness is folded into H-009 (the `(rho_k,C_k)`
asymptotics question), not opened as a new hypothesis, since it is a refinement of that question,
not a new one.

### B1/B2: investigated in full by an Opus subagent -- both dead as literally stated, one real byproduct

Full scratch code preserved at
`/tmp/claude-1000/-home-rat-weak-covering-conjecture/a8d2d60e-9133-436f-a13c-a0fb27ee7018/scratchpad/wcc/`
(not in this repo). Ran in parallel with the A3 subagent; C2's result (H-002's GAP C) is recorded in
`notes/H-002.md` instead.

**Finding 0 (framing, verified independently this session before trusting anything built on it):**
a Tao-cost budget dictionary, `j*(l) = B*(l) - l` where `B*(l) := max_z min{A(a) : Syrac_l(a)=z}`,
follows from an object identity this project already has (`R_{j-1,j}` is a unit multiple of the
Syrac-reachable set at cost `<=` some budget). The subagent computed `B*(l)` via an independent DP
and got an exact match to the known `j*(l)` table for all of `l=2..14` (13/13); this session
independently re-derived the same identity algebraically and confirmed the match extends to the full
known table `l=1..23` using only the already-known `j*(l)` values (no new computation needed for
that extension, since `j*(l)-l = B*(l)-2l` is pure algebra). Solid; not in dispute.

**B1 (endpoint-conditioned Doob bridge, target `O(sqrt l)`): dead, ~90% confidence.** `h_r(x)`
worked out in closed recursive form; the Birkhoff/Hilbert-metric machinery is technically applicable
(kernel strictly positive) but contributes nothing beyond the trivial `3^r` digit-truncation fact
already in the project (Tao's own Lemma 1.12). The load-bearing uniformity claim ("critical mean,
`O(1)` uniformly in `z`") is refuted directly: at `l=10`, conditioned means range over roughly `[-9,
+9]` around `2l`, and 77% of residues have no witness at all below the critical budget `1.7925*l`,
so no amount of conditioning reaches it for those residues. One salvageable, honest byproduct: the
trivial inequality `j*(l) <= max_z E[A|z] - l` is valid, and `max_z E[A|z]-2l` fits `~2.8*sqrt(l)`
better than log or linear on `l<=11` (weak evidence, small range, not a proof) -- if that growth rate
held asymptotically it would eventually beat the proven `(119/104)*l` bound (crossover around
`l~377`), but this is an extrapolated fit from `l<=11`, explicitly not a verified inequality, and
not pursued further.

**B2 (uniform local limit theorem in the fiber, target `O(log l)`): dead as stated, ~85%
confidence.** Its first statement is shown to be an exact restatement of Tao's own `beta=1`
conjecture with only polynomial loss allowed, strictly stronger than what's needed and no more
tractable. Its second statement (uniform local limit theorem) is refuted directly: `z=-1 mod 3^l`
(the all-ones tuple) has a badly non-uniform cost distribution at every `l` tested, growing worse
with `l`. Genuinely useful correction found along the way: `w=1/2` is the wrong evaluation point for
the fiber polynomial; the critical tilt is `w_c` solving `w_c/(1-w_c)=log_4(3)` (`w_c=0.442114...`,
checked exactly, `sum_z Z_{l,z}(w_c) = (log_4 3)^l` to 9 digits for `l=2..11`). Repeating the
analysis at `w_c` instead of `1/2` gives numbers at least consistent with the needed bound, but this
reduces WCC to "beta=1 at a subcritical tilt," trading one open uniform-equidistribution conjecture
for another, not resolving anything.

**The subagent's own flagged "cap" argument, sent to Gemini for a second opinion (Rule 11b) and
rejected.** The subagent noted that, since `e(l) = (j*(l)-l) + (1-log_4(3))*l` exactly, and
`j*(l)-l` stays in the narrow range `{2,...,5}` across the ENTIRE known table `l=1..23`
(non-monotonically: rises to 5 at `l=10-15`, back to 4 at `l=16-23`) with no visible growth, nearly
all of `e(l)`'s observed growth is mechanically coming from the `0.2075*l` linear term rather than
from `j*(l)-l` growing -- which, if it persisted, would point toward `e(l)` eventually LINEAR, the
opposite reading from the `rho_k -> log_4(3)` extrapolation this project has been carrying. Sent to
Gemini (`gemini-3.6-flash`, independent vendor) with the full argument and both this session's own
verification and A3's rho_k-gap finding as context. **Gemini's verdict: 10% confidence this is a
real signal, 90% a small-sample/discretization artifact**, with a specific mechanism: `j*(l)` is
integer-valued and its typical step is `+1` (since `log_4(3)<1` means the "natural" continuous
increment per unit `l` is less than 1, but the actual increment can only be an integer, so `+1` is
the generic step and `j*(l)-l` mechanically stays flat whenever that generic step occurs, by
construction, carrying no information about the true asymptotic regime); over a range as short as
`l<=23` this floor/discretization effect alone easily produces an apparently flat `j*(l)-l` under
any of the bounded/log/sqrt/slow-linear hypotheses, so the observation cannot distinguish between
them. Also warned explicitly against combining this with A3's `rho_k`-gap finding as "two
converging lines of evidence": the two are not independent (both ultimately trace back to the same
small-`l` regime) and treating them as mutually reinforcing would be an overreach. Gemini's suggested
real test, not yet run: compare the MEAN (not just the max) of `A_l(z)` across residues to `B*(l)`;
if the mean already tracks `1.7925*l` while only the max needs `2l+O(1)`, that would point to an
extreme-value-theory explanation for `e(l)` (favoring `O(log l)` or `O(sqrt l)` tail correction),
the opposite conclusion from the rejected "cap" reading. Not run this round; flagged as the next
concrete step if this line is picked up again, not chased further given the length of this round.

**Net effect on H-009 / GAP B**: no working route to `O(sqrt l)` or `O(log l)` found this round; both
B1 and B2 die on contact with an explicit uniformity requirement that the actual per-residue data
badly violates. The "cap" argument's suggestive alternative (linear `e(l)`) does not survive
independent review either, so this round leaves the bounded/log/sqrt/slow-linear question exactly
where it stood before it started: genuinely open, no direction eliminated with confidence, none
newly favored with confidence.

### Retroactive triage of round 23's search candidates (2026-07-30): a real methodological gap, closed

The researcher pointed out, correctly, that round 23's literature search (103 unique arXiv
candidates, plus OpenAlex/CrossRef hits) had mostly been filtered by title/query-context alone;
only a handful were actually opened and read. A second Opus subagent did a proper retroactive
pass: every arXiv candidate's real abstract read (not just its title), full PDF pulled and
introduction read for 11 where the abstract left the call ambiguous, all 176 unique OpenAlex works
and 16 CrossRef items from the citing-Tao and predecessor-set queries replayed and checked (the
logs recorded only counts, not IDs, so this needed redoing from scratch). Two DOIs independently
spot-checked against CrossRef and one arXiv ID against its abstract page before trusting any of
this (Rule 8c): all matched exactly as reported.

**Six new literature entries, L-067 through L-072** (full detail in `literature/INDEX.md`, notes
for L-067/L-068 in `literature/notes/`). The most substantive: **L-067** (Siegel,
arXiv:2007.15936, published version 2024) independently corroborates, in print and four years
earlier, the exact object identity round 23 established computationally (a "numen" chi_3 whose
mod-3^n projections are Tao's Syracuse random variables, with Prop. 1.17 and Lemma 1.12 rederived
as decay/functional-equation statements of the same object) -- no new bound, pure corroboration
and an earlier citation than Tao's own paper for this framing. **L-068** (Eliahou &
Verger-Gaugry, Comptes Rendus Math. 363 (2025), peer-reviewed, verified against CrossRef) is the
closest published, peer-reviewed neighbor of `R_{j,k}` found in the entire search: a mixed-base
2-and-3 numeration system `2^{k+1}n = sum a_i 3^i 2^{k-i}` with the SAME structural shape, but
weight tied to a FIXED position with free digits, the exact opposite of `R_{j,k}`'s fixed digits
with free, strictly-decreasing exponents -- so it does not touch the rank-coupling obstruction,
but is a genuine two-way citation (Rule 10) either way. L-069/L-070/L-071 are recorded,
appropriately labeled analogy-only or unread, specifically so a future round does not rediscover
the same vocabulary-adjacent clusters (rational-base numeration, non-archimedean Garsia entropy,
branching-random-walk minimal displacement) and mistake proximity of terms for applicability.
L-072's bibliographic details are confirmed against CrossRef; its content remains unverified, no
free copy found. L-066's venue is now confirmed (Wirsching's own institutional record identifies
it as a peer-reviewed contributed chapter, Vienna 1996 conference proceedings), clearing it for
citation once actually obtained; still unread, still pending the researcher's direct contact with
Wirsching.

**Nothing overturns an existing conclusion.** Two prior verdicts were re-checked against primary
sources rather than taken on faith and both survive: round 23's dismissal of the Christ-Li-Tao-
Thiele multilinear-oscillatory-integral framework (its "nondegenerate" phase condition is
precisely the independent-coordinates situation this project's rank coupling violates, confirmed
by reading `math/0311039` directly, not inferred), and the finding that Tao's citing-paper set (141
works) holds nothing bearing on `S(t)`. One candidate (`2607.02132`, a zero-sum-cover paper whose
abstract mentioned "coverings of a group by kernels") looked plausible enough to read in full;
turned out to use the opposite quantifier from Wirsching's covering (worst-case-over-sequences vs.
this project's existence-of-a-tuple), the same mismatch already on record for L-015.

**Negative coverage, condensed** (full per-paper log kept outside the repository, per the
subagent's own note, to avoid bloating this file with ~100 one-line dismissals): the 101 rejected
arXiv candidates group into greedoid/rank-keyword-collision, Mahonian q-analog rank statistics
never evaluated at roots of unity, per-element (not rank-coupled) Davenport-constant weights,
abstract numeration systems with no residue/Collatz content, the Christ-Li-Tao-Thiele cluster,
Garsia-entropy/self-similar-measure papers, branching random walks, shift radix systems,
average-cost MDP/homogenization, random energy/spin-glass models, plethysm/RSK/vertex operators,
and priority encoders. **The single most informative negative**: OpenAlex full-text search for
`"rank-dependent weight"` together with `"exponential sum"` returns zero mathematical hits (five
unrelated hits: killer-whale recognition, prospect theory, tail entropy, a QUBO paper, diabetes
omics). The rank-coupling obstruction this project keeps hitting has no established name in the
literature to search under -- a real, structural reason for 22+ rounds of empty searches, not
evidence the search itself was inadequate.

### A2 follow-up (2026-07-30): a genuinely coherent low-occupancy branch exists, but is not periodic and not shared across l

A2 proposed hunting for "an eventually periodic digit branch" whose occupancy stays
persistently rare as depth grows, distinct from `shallow_cylinder_occupancy.py`'s independent
argmin at each `r` (which could in principle jump between unrelated branches at each level).
Checked directly (`experiments/E-005-round24-checks/coherent_branch_search.py`): compute the
full occupancy array once at the deepest `r` tested, derive every shallower level from it by
aggregation (exact, same trick as the existing script), then trace the deepest argmin's
ancestors back through every level.

**A real, coherent branch exists.** At `l=16,20,23`, the branch that is the global argmin at
`r=12` (extended to `r=14` for `l=23`) is at or within rank 4 of the minimum at EVERY
shallower depth tested, not scattered independent minima. This confirms A2's premise (that a
single persistently-rare branch, not just a series of unrelated per-depth minima, is a real
structural feature of this system).

**But it is not what Codex specifically guessed.** The branch's base-3 digit sequence shows no
periodicity (checked by inspection, `l=23`: `1,0,1,0,0,0,2,1,0,2,1,1,...`, no repeating block
across 12-14 digits), so the "eventually periodic branch" mechanism is not what produces the
coherence. The branch is also `l`-specific, not a single universal path: the `l=16`, `l=20`,
and `l=23` argmin branches' digit sequences agree only trivially at `r=1` (where there are just
two candidate digits) and diverge completely from `r=2` on.

**Does not change any existing conclusion.** A coherent worst branch existing is fully
compatible with the already-established rollover finding (`-log(q_{l,r})` grows sub-linearly
in `r`, confirmed earlier this round): persistence of the SAME branch says nothing about how
fast its occupancy decays, which is the separate question the rollover test already answered
(no sign of exponential-in-l decay). Recorded as a real structural refinement, not a new lead;
A2 remains closed (no refutation signal found), B3 remains closed (no sharper lower bound
found).

### A1: a genuine follow-up attempt (Codex, gpt-5.6-sol, max reasoning), given the corrected identity -- the original transversal idea is now proven not to work, but a precise conditional reduction survives

Full prompt/transcript at `/home/rat/.claude/jobs/a8d2d60e/tmp/codex_a1_transversal_{prompt,transcript,lastmsg}.txt`.
Given A1's corrected concatenation identity (this round's earlier section: `A(u)` must include the
join gap, a genuine three-way prefix/join/suffix decomposition) and asked to actually attempt the
construction or find precisely why it fails, not just re-audit. Three of its claims independently
verified by hand before recording anything (Rule 8c):

1. **`G(gaps) mod 9` can only ever be `4` or `7`, never `1`** (confirmed, 0 violations in 12000
   random trials across `n=2..7`): the entire residue cylinder `1 mod 9` is unreachable by `G` at
   ANY budget, not merely under-represented. This directly kills the literal reading of A1's
   "sparse in every carry cylinder" premise: sparsity is false there, it's total exclusion.
2. **`Gamma_{n,4n-6} = Sigma_n`**, which Codex supported with an explicit inductive proof (not
   just a numerical claim), whose CONCLUSION was independently confirmed exactly by brute force
   for `n=2..6` (sizes match exactly at every `n`, not just containment). **Precision note, 2026-07-30
   (critique round)**: the brute-force check confirms the conclusion at six values of `n`; the
   inductive step itself was not independently re-derived line by line by this project, so "an
   unconditional covering theorem" should be read as "Codex's proof, whose conclusion checks out
   everywhere tested," not as a claim this project verified end to end.
3. **A max-phase-gap statistic `D_n`**, defined via an explicit normalized recurrence, independently
   recomputed from scratch (fixing a sign/division bug in a first attempt before trusting the
   result) and matching Codex's claimed sequence `2,6,9,11,14,16,18,20,22` for `n=2..10` exactly.

**The original transversal inference is now proven false, not merely unverified.** A clean counting
argument: with prefix-plus-join budget `O(r)`, the number of reachable absorber images is at most
`3^{O(r)}` (a `binom(D,r)` count), while a depth-`q` cylinder has size `3^{n-q}`. For `r=o(l)`, this
can only force a hit in cylinders of depth `q >= n - O(r)`, i.e. almost full depth -- exactly the
depth this construction was trying to avoid needing. A "density bound plus one representative per
cylinder" argument cannot work at any budget below nearly the full modulus; a hereditary-sparsity
statement, even if provable, would not license the intended conclusion.

**What survives: a genuine conditional reduction.** Define `H_{n,Q}` as the maximum gap, in the
`log_4`-phase orbit of the group `(Z/3^n)^* ` (proven cyclic of order `2*3^(n-1)`, generated by `4`
via LTE, `ord(4)=3^(n-1)`), between consecutive points reachable by the bulk object at total gap-cost
`<= Q`. A one-line prefix (`r=1`) plus a free join gap suffices to reduce covering entirely to
bounding this one bulk quantity: **if `H_{l-1,Q} = o(l)` at budget `Q` within `o(l)` of the WCC
threshold, that proves WCC outright.** This converts A1's vague "sparse in every cylinder" hope into
a single, crisp, checkable target -- but no existing bound gets anywhere near what is needed; proving
`H_{n,Q}=o(l)` near the critical budget would itself be the whole theorem.

**Consistency check, not a new result**: the one case Codex could prove unconditionally
(`Gamma_{n,4n-6}=Sigma_n`, giving `H_{n,4n-6}=2`) reproduces, via this completely independent
route, `j*(l) <= 3l-2` -- exactly the bound already verified two other ways in rounds 16-17
(brute-force enumeration and a block-separated 3-adic radix construction). Three independent
constructions now agree on the same bound; none comes close to the proven `(119/104)*l` from
E-003's mean-payoff solver, so this changes no numeric result, but the agreement is a real,
useful cross-check on the new machinery's correctness.

**Net effect on A1**: closed as originally proposed (the transversal argument does not work, proven
not assumed), but reopened in a sharper, well-defined form as a genuine open question (bound
`H_{n,Q}` near the critical budget) rather than left as a vague 30%-confidence hope. Not pursued
further this round; flagged for anyone who wants to attack `H_{n,Q}` directly, which is now a
concrete, self-contained combinatorial target on the phase orbit of a cyclic group, decoupled from
the rest of H-003's rank-coupling apparatus.

# 2026-07-30 (twenty-fifth round): parallel generation round, Codex and Gemini, given the full
# post-critique state (corrected H-009 exponent, A1's H_{n,Q} reduction, A3's impossibility proof)

10 suggestions requested from each, spread across (a) direct WCC proof/disproof, (b) pinning
e(l)'s rate, (c) attacking `H_{n,Q}` directly. Full prompt/transcripts at
`/home/rat/.claude/jobs/a8d2d60e/tmp/{codex,gemini}_round25_*`.

## Verification done before recording anything (Rule 8c/11)

**A real object-confusion caught in Gemini's entire angle-(c) cluster (items 1-3, including its
own highest-confidence item at 90%).** All three treat `H_{n,Q}` as if it were about gaps in the
simple linear orbit `{4^k mod 3^n : k=0..Q-1}` (a set of size exactly `Q`), inviting the classical
Three Distance Theorem (Sos 1958) or subgroup-distribution/sum-product tools. But `H_{n,Q}` as
actually defined (A1's section above) is about `Gamma_{n,Q} = {G_n(gaps) : sum(gaps)<=Q}`, whose
SIZE is a sum of binomial coefficients (compositions of budgets up to `Q` into `n-1` parts), not
`Q` itself. Checked directly: at `n=10, Q=40`, `|Gamma_{n,Q}|` is at least 273 million, against a
naive linear orbit's 40 points, seven orders of magnitude apart. The two objects are not the same,
and a theorem about gaps in the small linear orbit does not obviously say anything about gaps in
the much richer, combinatorially-generated `Gamma_{n,Q}`. Gemini's own stated 90% confidence for
this specific conflation is not trustworthy; downgraded to "does not currently apply as stated"
pending a real translation of the theorem to the actual object, not attempted here.

**8 of Codex's citations independently checked against arXiv (author/title match)**: Marklof-
Strombergsson (1103.3152, "Diameters of random circulant graphs"), Addario-Berry-Reed (0712.2582,
"Minima in branching random walks"), Gouezel (math/0202147, decay-of-correlations operator-renewal
method), Bond-Levine (1309.3445, "Abelian networks I"), Mallein (1606.01748, BRW extremal-process
genealogy), Lev (math/0508179, critical pairs / Kemperman), Grynkiewicz (1709.09285, iterated
sumsets), Hare-Vavra (2307.07375, self-similar sets/measures in the p-adics). All real, all match
the described topic. Not independently checked: Adler-Coppersmith-Hassner (state-splitting, a
well-known 1983 IEEE Trans. IT paper, high prior confidence but not re-verified this round),
Kannan-Lovasz and Banaszczyk (covering-minima/transference, both well-known, not re-verified).

**Codex's own item 1 reuses this project's own verified apparatus correctly**: its composition
formula `F_d(x) = 1 + 3*2^{-d}*x` matches exactly the affine-contraction-semigroup identity
already verified in round 18 (H-008(b)), a good sign it engaged with the actual problem rather
than pattern-matching a generic template.

## Assessment: Codex's response is more trustworthy this round

Every Codex item states an explicit, checkable collapse/discard condition ("if X, this reduces to
the closed route and should be discarded") -- a mark of real engagement, not confident-sounding
generation. Gemini's response has no comparable self-checks and its highest-confidence cluster
turned out to rest on an object confusion caught within minutes of direct computation. This does

and both assert specific formulas with the same confident-but-unsubstantiated flavor this project
has caught before (e.g. round 20's confabulated `rho_k` formula); treat as unverified leads, not
findings, same bar as everything else.
