### Same-day follow-up: testing the cheap round-25 items directly

Codex's own item 6 came with an explicit go/no-go: "enumerate all local two-move diamonds
[adjacent-rank increments `s_i -> s_i+1` in the exponent representation]; if they are confluent,
invoke abelian-networks least-action formalism; if the diamond test fails, this route should be
abandoned." **Tested directly** (`experiments/E-005-round24-checks/round25_diamond_test.py`):
FAILS. In 4995/20000 random trials the two orders of applying "increment rank `i`" then
"increment rank `i+1`" are not both legal or both illegal. Concrete verified example:
`s=[11,10,6,5,4,2]`, incrementing rank 2 (`6->7`) then rank 3 (`5->6`) succeeds; incrementing
rank 3 first collides immediately with rank 2's still-unchanged value (`6>=6`, illegal). The
mechanism: incrementing the lower-index rank first frees room the higher-index rank then needs,
not vice versa. Per Codex's own stated condition, item 6 is closed: not confluent, the
abelian-networks reduction does not apply as proposed. Cheap, honest negative result, consistent
with this round's general pattern (real mechanisms, real failure conditions, most collapse on
contact -- which is what a mature obstruction after 25 rounds should look like).

**Item 1 (weighted 3-adic path-set fractal / transducer entropy) tested via a shortcut**: rather
than building Codex's proposed truncated transfer-operator machinery, computed the quantity it
targets directly and exactly: the Shannon entropy per symbol of the (normalized) per-residue
tuple-count distribution at the true critical budget `j*(l)`, reusing the already-validated exact
DP (`experiments/E-005-round24-checks/round25_entropy_test.py`), `l=4..16`.

**Result: the entropy deficit from `log(3)` SHRINKS as `l` grows** (`0.131` at `l=4` down to
`~0.046` at `l=15-16`), not staying at a persistent gap. This is evidence AGAINST item 1's
proposed disproof mechanism, which needed a persistent `(1-delta)*log(3)` ceiling to conclude
"only `3^{(1-delta)n}` cylinders occur." A power-law fit of the deficit over the accessible range
(`l=8..15`) gives an exponent of `~0.75-0.78` (deficit `~ C/l^0.78`), decaying slower than `1/l`
but faster than `1/sqrt(l)`. **Flagged with the same caution this session already learned the
hard way once this round** (the rollover-test correction above): a clean-looking small-`l` power
law (`l<=16` here) is exactly the shape of pattern that turned out to be a shallow-regime
artifact earlier in this same round, and this has not been pushed further or independently
reviewed. Recorded as a data point, not a finding about `e(l)`'s growth rate; not connected to
H-009's own exponent estimate without a real derivation linking the two quantities, which was not
attempted here.

**Net effect on item 1**: the specific disproof mechanism proposed does not find support (entropy
approaches, not stays below, `log(3)`), closing the disproof reading. The entropy-deficit-decay
observation itself is left as an open, unverified curiosity, not built on further.

**Item 10 (nonzero 3-adic discrete degree), naive reading tested**: item 10's actual proposal
(a SAT search for a "connected cubical chain" with nonzero topological degree over grouped
macro-cells of `b` ranks) was not implemented -- Codex's own lowest-confidence item (21%) and
genuinely underspecified beyond a first read; a SAT solver (`python-sat`, `pysat`) was installed
this round in case it was needed. Before committing to that build, tested the much cheaper
NECESSARY condition it implies: is the raw per-residue tuple count, at the true critical budget
`j*(l)`, ever divisible by 3 (`experiments/E-005-round24-checks/round25_degree_test.py`)? **Yes,
routinely**: at every `l=4..13` tested, a stable `~1/3` of residues (not drifting: `0.296, 0.303,
0.337, 0.329, 0.335, 0.328, 0.332, 0.334, 0.334, 0.334`) have tuple count divisible by 3. The raw
unsigned tuple count is not a "nonzero mod 3" invariant. This does not rule out a genuinely
different, SIGNED/topological degree notion (which could differ from the raw count), but the
simplest, most direct reading of item 10's premise fails cleanly. Recorded as a real, if partial,
negative result; the sophisticated macro-cell SAT construction itself remains unattempted, both
too underspecified and too low-confidence to justify the implementation effort this round.

**Item 4 (genealogy of hard residues as a typed branching walk), a bounded first pass**: Codex's
own highest-confidence item (58%). Its full proposal (a complete decision-diagram over `z ->
c_n(z)` with branching-process statistics extracted from the whole frontier) needs either a
smarter analytic/transfer-operator approach or far more exact data points than are currently
reachable. Took the much smaller first step of tracking only the single champion (the residue
achieving `B*(l)`, i.e. determining `j*(l)`): does it descend from the previous level's champion
(`argmax_z(l+1) mod 3^l == argmax_z(l)`), or does the "hardest residue" switch lineage between
levels (`experiments/E-005-round24-checks/round25_genealogy_test.py`, `l=4..12`, 8 transitions)?

**Result: switches in 6 of 8 transitions** (only `l=8->9` and `l=10->11` stay on the same branch).
Genuinely informative (rules out a naive single-persistent-champion picture, consistent with real
branching-process-style volatility rather than a smooth deterministic frontier), but far too
little data (8 transitions) to discriminate between the three regimes item 4 named (log-corrected,
finite-variance `sqrt(n)`, or stable/heavy-tailed). **Recorded as attempted but genuinely
inconclusive given the data ceiling**, not as a dead end and not as a resolved finding; the
per-residue full-cost computation this needs is only practical to about `l=12-13` before cost
balloons (matching `mean_cost_per_residue.py`'s own documented ceiling), an order of magnitude too
little range for real branching-process statistics. Would need either a genuinely cleverer
computational shortcut or an analytic argument, neither attempted here.

**Item 5 (tropical renewal equation near the neutral loop z=-2), premise tested directly**:
Codex's proposal was to induce the Bellman dynamics away from the fixed point `z=-2` (`T_0(-2)=-2`
exactly, verified via LTE in round 24's A3 work) and run a min-plus/tropical renewal analysis on
the excursions away from it, treating `-2` as a "neutral loop" the optimal trajectory keeps
returning to and lingering near. This premise is checkable directly from the already-solved,
already-committed mean-payoff-game certificates (`certificate_k{10,11,12,13}.json`, which store
the converged `sigma` (minimizer's optimal digit per state), `lam` (long-run value, constant `=
rho_k` at convergence), and `h` (bias term) for every state).

At `z=-2 mod 3^k`, the minimizer's optimal choice is `sigma[-2]=0` at every tested `k`, and one of
the three legal extensions of the adversary's move (`e=2`) is exactly the zero-cost self-loop back
to `-2` itself (matching the algebraic fixed point). Whether the dynamics actually linger there
under optimal play depends on whether the ADVERSARY, who picks `e` to maximize the game's value
(tie-break `argmax(lam, h)`, the same convention already used by `mpg3.py`'s
`gain_bias_of_action`), ever actually chooses that self-loop.

**Result (`experiments/E-005-round24-checks/round25_item5_neutral_loop_test.py`, k=10,11,12,13,
all four checked exactly from the stored certificates, not simulated): NO, at every k.** The
adversary strictly prefers moving away (`h` strictly higher at the chosen successor than at the
self-loop, in all four cases: e.g. k=10, `h=-8` at the chosen move vs. `h=-83/9` at the self-loop).
The zero-cost self-loop at the fixed point is a real edge in the game graph, present at every
tested window size, but it is never the adversary's best response, so it is never used under
optimal play. There is no state where the optimal trajectory sits at or keeps returning to `-2`;
the point is visited, structurally, as any unit must pass near it, but immediately departed. This
directly refutes the premise item 5 needs (a sticky neutral loop worth anchoring a renewal analysis
to), at the level Codex itself flagged as a collapse condition. It does not rule out an
excursion/renewal analysis anchored to some OTHER recurrent structure in the game, only the
specific `z=-2` anchor Codex proposed. An earlier, less careful attempt at this same question (a
300-step ad hoc simulation from `z=-2` using the k=10 certificate) showed the trajectory departing
`-2` immediately and a mean cost over the run (1.1967) somewhat below `rho=11/9=1.2222`; that
discrepancy is most likely a short-window/transient artifact (a free zero-cost run of length `k=10`
from the exact fixed point before the state space "wraps," pulling the windowed average down) and
was not pursued further once the direct certificate check above gave a clean, exact answer to the
actual question at stake.

**Work-list item 8 (Gemini #4/#5, angle b), the two items flagged as "needs independent
verification of a specific claimed formula before trusting at all"**:

*Gemini #5, "Boundary Minkowski Dimension of Neutral 3-Adic Trajectories"* claims a formula
`p = 1 - d_F`, connecting H-009's empirically-fitted convergence exponent `p` (from
`rho_k - log_4(3) ~ A/k^p`, `p~0.84-0.90`) to the Minkowski/Hausdorff dimension `d_F` of the set of
"neutral (non-contracting) boundary trajectories" of `T(x)=(3x+1)/2^{v_2(3x+1)}` in `Z_3`, via the
Jenkinson-Pollicott algorithm. The citation itself checks out: Jenkinson and Pollicott's method
(originally 2001/2002, refined 2018, arXiv:1611.09276) is a real, well-established technique for
computing Hausdorff dimension of dynamically defined Cantor sets from periodic orbits and a
transfer operator on a Hardy space, confirmed via web search against the actual paper abstracts.
What is NOT confirmed: the specific bridge formula `p = 1 - d_F` connecting that machinery to this
project's window-convergence exponent. Two targeted searches for any general "finite-window /
finite-memory approximation exponent equals one minus a Hausdorff dimension" result in the
subshift-of-finite-type or thermodynamic-formalism literature found nothing supporting it. This
project's own same-day item-5 (Codex) finding above adds a further, independent reason for doubt:
the "neutral" fixed point `z=-2` this formula's boundary set is built around is NOT dynamically
sticky under the actual optimal game policy (the adversary immediately departs it, verified exactly
at k=10..13), which undercuts the premise that trajectories near `-2` are a dynamically special
"boundary" carrying the field's fractal structure. **Recorded as unverified and not trusted**, per
Rule 11: a real citation reused for an unsupported quantitative claim, the same failure shape as
Gemini's angle-(c) `H_{n,Q}` object-confusion earlier this round, not independently re-derived here
and not built on further.

*Gemini #4, "Extreme Value Theory for Log-Correlated Fields" (Bramson / Fyodorov-Hiary-Keating)*
claims the per-residue cost landscape `Cost(x) = min{j : x in R_{j-1,j} mod 3^l}` is a log-correlated
random field over the 3-adic tree, `Cov(Cost(x),Cost(y)) ~ A*(l - v_3(x-y))`, which if true would
predict a specific `-3/(2*theta)*log(l)` correction to `j*(l)`'s leading `mu*l` term (Bramson's
theorem). Unlike item 5, this premise is a directly checkable empirical claim, and this project
already has the machinery (`core.py`'s validated joint DP, reused as in `round25_genealogy_test.py`)
to test it without new theory. Tested via Monte Carlo sampling of residue pairs binned by
`v_3(x-y)` (`experiments/E-005-round24-checks/round25_item4_gemini_logcorrelated_test.py`), l=10
and l=12 (200000 sampled pairs each):

```
        l=10                          l=12
 v3   l-v  n_pairs   Cov       v3   l-v  n_pairs   Cov
  0    10   99820   -0.2683      0   12  100196   -0.2811
  1     9   67013   -0.2743      1   11   66383   -0.2502
  2     8   22206    1.0197      2   10   22200    1.0678
  3     7    7362    1.7039      3    9    7487    1.6236
  4     6    2404    2.1242      4    8    2475    1.9742
  5     5     810    2.2221      5    7     828    2.5751
  6     4     253    2.3469      6    6     274    2.5574
  7     3      89    2.6427      7    5     110    2.6722
  8     2      31    2.7667      8    4     35    2.0546
```

Two things this rules out cleanly. First, `Cov` is not linear in `l-v` as the log-correlated
hypothesis needs: it plateaus (flat, mildly negative, `~-0.27`) at `v=0,1` instead of continuing to
grow with `l-v`, and grows unevenly, not linearly, for `v>=2`. Second, and more directly: `Cov` at
fixed `v` is essentially IDENTICAL between `l=10` and `l=12` (e.g. `v=4`: `2.12` vs `1.97`; `v=6`:
`2.35` vs `2.56`), not scaling with `l` the way a genuine depth-`l` log-correlated tree field
requires (correlation between two leaves sharing a depth-`v` prefix should grow with the remaining
depth `l-v`, i.e. with `l` itself for fixed `v`, since more of the tree lies between them and the
leaves). What is observed instead, correlation depending on the 3-adic distance `v_3(x-y)` alone
and not on `l`, is a translation-invariant, finite-range correlation structure, the same qualitative
shape as the window-k game construction this project's own best results are built on (states more
than `k` digits apart are treated as conditionally near-independent by that framework). **This is
real evidence against the specific log-correlated-field premise item 4 needs**; it does not rule
out every possible extreme-value correction (a bounded, translation-invariant short-range
correlation structure could still produce some other, real correction term), only the specific
Bramson/FHK log-correlated-tree mechanism named, which needs correlation to scale with depth and
does not, at least at `l=10,12`.

**Gemini's remaining four items (6, 7, 8, 9, 10), tested or closed by direct reasoning**:

*Item 7 ("SAT/SMT Counterexample Mining for l=24..30")*: not a genuinely new avenue. Its own text
admits a literal SAT encoding at that scale (`3^30`) is computationally infeasible; the actual
exact-covering extension past `l=23` is already H-001's own DP-based approach (in progress, `l=24`
relaunched after an earlier unrelated crash). Not separately attempted; superseded by existing work.

*Item 8 ("3-Adic Syracuse Attractor Cycles")*: proposes lifting the `z=-2` fixed point to a family
`r_l mod 3^l` and checking whether its required budget exceeds `log_4(3)*l + O(sqrt(l))`, as a
constructive disproof seed. Directly testable, and cheap, reusing `core.py`'s joint DP
(`experiments/E-005-round24-checks/round25_item8_gemini_syracuse_cycle_test.py`). One real
bookkeeping trap caught before trusting the result: `core.py`'s cost is in Tao's A/B-scale
(`B(z)`), not Wirsching's `j`-scale; this project already found and fixed exactly this scale
mismatch once before (H-002's C1 finding), and a first, uncorrected pass here reproduced the same
error (`B(-2)=11` at `l=4` looked like it exceeded the true max `j*(4)=7`, which is definitionally
impossible since `j*(4)` IS the max over all residues, an internal-consistency red flag caught
immediately per Rule 8c). Converting `j(z) = B(z) - l` before comparing:

```
 l  j(-2)  j*(l) (true max)  is -2 the argmax?  threshold=l*log4(3)+sqrt(l)  j(-2)>threshold?
 4    7            7               True                  5.170                YES
 5    8            9               False                 6.198                YES
 6    9           10               False                 7.204                YES
 7   10           11               False                 8.193                YES
 8   10           12               False                 9.168                YES
 9   10           13               False                10.132                no
10   12           15               False                11.087                YES
11   12           16               False                12.034                no
12   12           17               False                12.974                no
```

`z=-2` is genuinely the global worst residue at `l=4` (`j(-2)=j*(4)=7` exactly), a real, striking
small-`l` fact consistent with `-2`'s special role elsewhere in this project (the `T_0` fixed point,
A3's diamond-property proof). But the pattern does not hold up as `l` grows: `j(-2)/j*(l)` declines
monotonically from `1.0` at `l=4` to `0.706` at `l=12`, and whether `j(-2)` clears the stated
threshold flips inconsistently (yes at `l=4,5,6,7,8,10`, no at `l=9,11,12`) rather than clearing it
by a growing margin as a genuine `(1+epsilon)` linear-excess disproof would need. **This is the
same shape of pattern this session already learned to distrust once this round** (the B3 rollover
correction): a striking small-`l` coincidence that fades rather than strengthens. Closed as a
disproof lead; `-2` is not tracking the true worst-case residue at the `l` values reachable here.

*Item 9 ("Continuous Order-Simplex Geometry and Isoperimetric Pushforward Measures")*: proposes
that if `Vol(Delta_{j,l}) >> 3^l*log(3^l)` at the critical budget, Milman's isoperimetric machinery
forces the pushforward measure to cover. Checked the basic scale condition directly (exact integer
arithmetic, `|R_{j*-1,j*}| = C(j*(l)+l, j*(l))` against `3^l*ln(3^l)`, `l=1..23`, reusing the
already-known exact table): the ratio grows from `~0.6-0.9` at `l=1..4` to `~45` at `l=23`,
comfortably and increasingly satisfying the stated scale requirement. Unlike every item tested so
far this round, **item 9 passes its own basic necessary-scale check rather than failing on
contact**. This does not confirm the actual claim (a positive, uniformly bounded-below Cheeger
constant for the specific pushforward measure, the load-bearing and much deeper part, not attempted
here), but it means the proposal is not obviously dead on the cheapest possible test, unlike several
others this round. Left as a genuinely open, moderately-more-credible-than-average item, not chased
further given the isoperimetric-geometry machinery it would need is substantial new work.

*Item 10 ("Structural Disproof via Dirichlet Character Kernel Invariants")*: closed by direct
reasoning, no computation needed. Two independent problems. First, the stated test ("rank(M) <
phi(3^l) implies a nontrivial character-kernel obstruction to covering") is true but empty: `M`'s
column space is spanned by character-evaluations at the DISTINCT reachable values, so
`rank(M) <= |{distinct v(S)}|`, meaning `rank(M) < phi(3^l)` is automatic and uninformative whenever
covering has not yet happened (which is exactly when it would be checked), and trivially `= phi(3^l)`
(full rank, no obstruction) once covering succeeds; the proposal is definitionally equivalent to the
covering question itself, offering no new leverage, not a novel computation. Second, the object
itself is under-specified relative to this project's actual structure: it asks for rank-ordered
subsets "of size `l`", but `R_{j,k}`'s defining data is `j+1` strictly decreasing exponents drawn
from `{0,...,j+k}`, not `l`-element subsets; this looks like a further instance of the same
citation-real-but-object-mismatched pattern already caught once this round (Gemini's `H_{n,Q}`
confusion). Closed without running the proposed SAT/cyclotomic-field computation, on both grounds.

*Item 6 ("Microcanonical Budget Transfer Operator without Fourier Characters")*: the most
Gemini-confident (60%) of the untested items, and the only one framed as a mechanism (a fugacity-
weighted transfer operator with no spatial Fourier variable) rather than a direct quantitative
claim. Built the finite-window truncation of the proposed operator directly
(`experiments/E-005-round24-checks/round25_item6_gemini_transfer_operator.py`, `x` restricted to
the low `n` 3-adic digits, `n=6,7,8`, i.e. mod `3^6` to `3^8`) and computed its two dominant
eigenvalues (dense LAPACK; `n=8`'s `6561x6561` matrix took ~125s per eigenvalue call, so its
`z`-grid was narrowed) at `z=0.40, 0.45, 0.49` approaching the item's own `z=1/2` point of interest:

```
 z      n=6      n=7      n=8
 0.40   0.0294   0.0220   0.0237
 0.45   0.0432   0.0310   0.0342
 0.49   0.0563   0.0429   0.0468
```

The spectral gap drops substantially (22-28%) from `n=6` to `n=7` at every `z` tested, consistent
with the item's own "degenerate" branch (gap vanishing as truncation grows). But it then partially
recovers (7-10%) from `n=7` to `n=8` at every `z`, not continuing to shrink. Three points with
non-monotonic behavior cannot distinguish a slowly-decaying finite-size correction from
stabilization at a genuine positive limit (the item's other, CLT-supporting branch); `n=9` would
cost roughly 27x longer per eigenvalue call (about an hour each) under this dense approach, past
what a bounded first look should spend without the researcher's own go-ahead. **Recorded honestly
as genuinely inconclusive**, unlike most of this round's items: it did not collapse cleanly on
contact, and it did not confirm a direction either.

## Round 25 summary

Of Codex's 3 "cheap, checkable" items: 2 closed (item 6, item 10's necessary condition both fail on
direct test), 1 gave a real but inconclusive negative-leaning signal (item 1, entropy deficit
shrinks, against the proposed disproof mechanism). Of Codex's "real investment" items, 2 given a
bounded first look: item 4 (inconclusive, data-limited) and item 5 (closed: the neutral-loop
premise fails exactly, checked directly against the solved game certificates at k=10..13). Codex
items 2, 3, 7, 8, 9 remain genuinely untested.

All 10 of Gemini's items now have some real disposition. Items 1-3 (angle-c `H_{n,Q}` cluster)
closed together via the object-confusion catch. Item 4 (Bramson/FHK log-correlated field) tested
directly and fails: correlation does not scale with `l` at fixed 3-adic distance the way the
mechanism requires. Item 5 (Jenkinson-Pollicott `p=1-d_F`) left unverified: real citation, no
literature support found for the specific formula, undercut by Codex item 5's own finding. Item 6
(fugacity transfer operator) tested numerically (`n=6,7,8` truncations): genuinely inconclusive,
non-monotonic spectral-gap behavior that neither collapses nor confirms a bounded gap. Item 7 (SAT
search `l=24..30`) not separately tested, redundant with H-001's own ongoing exact extension. Item
8 (`z=-2`-seeded Syracuse cycle family) tested directly: `-2` is the true global argmax at `l=4`
but its relative extremeness fades steadily through `l=12` (ratio to the true max drops `1.0` to
`0.706`), the same shallow-regime-coincidence shape this round already learned to distrust once.
Item 9 (isoperimetric pushforward measure) passes its own basic volume-scale necessary condition
(checked exactly, `l=1..23`, ratio growing from `~0.6` to `~45`), the only item this round to clear
even its cheapest test; the load-bearing Cheeger-constant claim itself remains untested, real new
geometric machinery. Item 10 (Dirichlet character kernel) closed by direct reasoning alone, no
computation needed: the stated rank-deficiency test is definitionally equivalent to "does not yet
cover," offering no new leverage, and separately mismatches this project's actual index structure.

None of the round's 20 combined suggestions (10 Codex + 10 Gemini) produced a new bound or a
resolved answer to GAP A/B/C. Final accounting: 12 of the 20 items now have a real, direct-test
result (Codex 1, 4, 5, 6, 10; Gemini 1, 2, 3, 4, 6, 8, 10); 2 more were partially checked without
a full test (Gemini 5's citation verified but formula untrusted; Gemini 9's basic scale condition
checked but its deeper claim untouched); 6 remain genuinely not attempted (Codex 2, 3, 7, 8, 9;
Gemini 7, the last redundant with existing work rather than simply unexamined). The round's honest
yield: one real object-confusion caught in Gemini's highest-confidence cluster, six clean negative
results (Codex 5, 6, 10; Gemini 4, 8, 10), one unverified/untrusted formula (Gemini 5), one item
passing its cheapest check without resolving the deeper claim (Gemini 9), two genuinely
inconclusive numerical results (Codex 4; Gemini 6), and one entropy observation weighing against a
disproof route without confirming anything positive (Codex 1). Consistent with this project's
now-repeated experience: after 25 rounds, most freshly generated ideas either collapse on contact
with a direct test or reduce to the same rank-coupling obstruction; genuinely novel, resolving
leverage keeps not appearing, which is itself the most honest thing this round of generation
established.

# 2026-07-31: deeper investigation of item 9 and the remaining open round-25 items, at the
# researcher's explicit request

## Item 9 (isoperimetric pushforward measure), deep dive: the fixed-constant threshold is refuted

The first pass only checked that `Vol(Delta_{j,l})` clears `3^l*log(3^l)` at the critical budget,
comfortably and with a growing margin. That is necessary but says nothing about whether the
threshold is actually TIGHT, i.e. whether SOME fixed constant `C` makes "`Vol > C*3^l*log(3^l)`
implies covering" a correct statement. Tested this directly
(`experiments/E-005-round24-checks/round25_item9_gemini_isoperimetric_deep_dive.py`, exact integer
arithmetic, `l=1..23`): compute the minimum `C` that would be needed for the threshold to correctly
predict "not yet covering" at `j=j*(l)-1` (the point right before covering actually happens).

```
 l   min C needed        l   min C needed
 5   0.964               15  7.849
 9   1.510               19  10.085
12   4.342               23  24.526
```

This minimum-`C` is NOT bounded: it grows from under 1 (small `l`) to `~24.5` by `l=23`, with no
sign of leveling off. **No single fixed constant makes item 9's stated threshold correct for all
`l`**: for large enough `l`, a fixed-`C` version of the criterion would incorrectly predict
covering strictly before it actually happens, by a widening margin. A second, independent way of
seeing the same gap: the excess budget the threshold implies over the pure pigeonhole bound
(`j_isoperi - j_pigeon`) stays flat at `3-4` across `l=5..23`, while the TRUE excess (`j*(l) -
j_pigeon`) climbs steadily from `4` to `9` over the same range.

This refutes item 9's claim exactly as stated (a fixed-constant volume criterion). It does not
refute a genuinely dimension-aware version of Milman's machinery, since `Delta_{j,l}`'s actual
dimension (`j*(l)+1`) grows with `l`, and real isoperimetric/concentration inequalities often carry
dimension-dependent constants that Gemini's stated formula omits entirely. That corrected, harder
version needs real new isoperimetric-geometry derivation, not a data check, and was not attempted.
What is now established: the specific, simplest reading of item 9 is empirically false, not merely
"not yet confirmed."

## The remaining Codex items (2, 3, 7, 8, 9), each given a real attempt

**Item 2 (periodic hard target + minimum-cycle-mean certificate)**: rather than build the full
product automaton Codex proposes, ran the cheap first step it implies: starting from `z=-2 mod 81`
(the `l=4` global-worst residue), greedily extend one digit at a time by picking, among the up to 3
children `z+t*3^l`, whichever has the highest cost at the next level
(`experiments/E-005-round24-checks/round25_item2_codex_periodic_target.py`, reusing `core.py`,
`l=4..13`, cost converted from Tao's B-scale to Wirsching's j-scale, the same conversion caught and
fixed in item 8's test below). **Result: cost(l) = l+4 exactly for every l in range, digit stream
`0,2,0,0,0,2,1,2,2`.** This is not a growing linear excess: it exactly reproduces the ALREADY
KNOWN, ALREADY Gemini-reviewed finding (this same round's separate B1/B2 follow-up, "the subagent's
own flagged cap argument" section above) that `j*(l)-l` stays bounded (`{2,...,5}`, non-monotonic)
across the whole known table and was independently assessed by Gemini as `90%` a small-sample
discretization artifact, not a real growth signal. The greedy trajectory tracks the true `j*(l)`
exactly while `j*(l)=l+4` (`l=5..9`), then falls exactly 1 behind once the true worst residue moves
to a different branch at `l=10` (`j*(l)=l+5`), consistent with the earlier round-25 genealogy-test
finding that champion lineages switch. No sign of unbounded growth; closed via the same mechanism
already flagged and reviewed, not a new disproof lead.

**Item 3 (delayed set-valued state-splitting encoder)**: closed by a direct theoretical argument,
no computation needed, using Codex's own stated collapse condition ("if every useful state
collapses to a singleton, or their value equals rho_k, discard"). The window-k game as already
formalized and solved (E-003) is a finite, perfect-information, two-player mean-payoff game whose
transition rule (`T_d(z+m*e) mod m`) depends only on the current state, not on history: a genuinely
Markov game. By the classical Ehrenfeucht-Mycielski theorem (mean-payoff games on finite graphs are
memoryless-determined: both players have optimal POSITIONAL strategies, independently verified via
web search against multiple academic sources; this is the same foundational fact already cited in
this project's own L-062, applied there to a different covering-radius problem), no strategy with
memory or delayed commitment can beat the value already achieved by the memoryless-optimal policy
`sigma` this project already computes. A set-state machine, however constructed, either (a) encodes
no more information than the current k-digit window, in which case it is provably bounded by
`rho_k` from below (Codex's own "discard" condition), or (b) secretly encodes information from
beyond the k-digit window, in which case it is really just `rho_k'` for some `k'>k` in disguise,
already known via this project's own established window monotonicity, not a new mechanism. Closed.

**Item 7 (H_{n,Q} as circulant-graph covering radius)**: rather than build Codex's lattice-covering
machinery (Kannan-Lovasz/Banaszczyk transference over a congruence lattice generated by "closed
words"), first measured `H_{n,Q}` itself directly, since `G_n` (A1's bulk quantity) turns out to be
exactly `core.py`'s already-validated `Syrac_n` (same recursive definition, `Gamma_{n,Q}` is the
support of `core.joint_counts(n,n,Q)` at cost `<=Q`), and the log_4-phase orbit lives on `P_n=<4>`,
the order-`3^(n-1)` cyclic subgroup of `(Z/3^nZ)^*` (`experiments/E-005-round24-checks/round25_item7_Hquantity_direct_measurement.py`).
Measured `H_{n,Q}` (the max circular gap among reached phases) at `n=8` across a range of budget
ratios `Q/n`:

```
 Q/n    H/n
 1.125  273.4
 1.5     16.75
 1.75     2.625
 2.0      0.625
 2.25     0.25
 2.5      0.125 (full saturation, H=1)
```

**`H/n` only drops below 1 once `Q/n` reaches about `2.0-2.25`, roughly DOUBLE the best proven
direct-covering ratio** (E-003's mean-payoff solver gives `119/104~1.1442`). At the actual proven
critical ratio, `H_{n,Q}` is empirically enormous (comparable to the full phase-circle size, not
`o(n)`). This is a real, if small-`n` (`n<=9` tested), confirmation of A1's own anticipated
difficulty ("proving `H_{n,Q}=o(l)` near the critical budget would itself be the whole theorem"):
the reduction does not appear to make the problem easier at reachable scales. Codex's lattice
machinery was not built on top of this: it would be chasing a quantity already shown, by direct
measurement, not to be small where it would need to be.

**Item 8 (ternary voltage gadgets in the tower)**: closed using item 7's data directly, no new
computation needed. Item 8 hopes for a gadget family costing `alpha*b+delta_b` per `b`-digit block
(`delta_b=o(b)`) that lifts every reachable point to all three fibers at each tower step, which
would propagate full phase coverage through the tower at a TOTAL budget ratio approaching `alpha`
(`~0.79` or, generously, the proven `~1.14`) as the tower grows (since `delta_b=o(b)` means the
per-block overhead vanishes in the rate). But item 7's direct measurement already shows that
achieving small `H` (full phase coverage) requires a budget ratio of `~2.0-2.25`, not `~0.79-1.14`,
using the FULL, UNRESTRICTED reachable set `Gamma_{n,Q}` -- a strict superset of what any specific
gadget construction can achieve, since a gadget family is a restricted subset of all reachable
paths. A restricted construction cannot do better than the full set; since the full set already
fails to achieve small `H` at ratio `~1.14`, no gadget family achieves it either. Item 8's hoped
`delta_b=o(b)` family cannot exist at the stated budget rate. Closed, no new computation needed
beyond item 7's.

**Item 9 (critical-pair theory on closed phase blocks)**: Codex's own stated go/no-go test is
whether the "phase cocycle" is additive under concatenation of same-state return words. Reasoned
through directly rather than simulated: the game/G_n transition is an AFFINE map (a multiplicative
part `2^{d+1}` composed with an additive "carry" shift), and for a closed loop returning exactly to
its starting state, the natural "phase" (the discrete log of the loop's accumulated MULTIPLICATIVE
coefficient, `prod(2^{d_i+1})`, the loop's linearized multiplier at its own fixed point) composes
under concatenation by the ordinary chain rule for iterated affine maps, independent of the
additive carry terms, which do not affect a periodic orbit's multiplier. **This suggests Codex's
own go/no-go gate is likely satisfied (does not collapse)**, essentially for structural/algebraic
reasons rather than anything specific to this problem. This was not independently verified by
direct simulation (unlike every other item this round), because doing so correctly requires
pinning down Codex's underspecified "boundary state" and "return word" definitions precisely enough
to avoid another object-confusion-style error, which was judged too risky to guess at reliably
within a bounded first look. **The actual hard content (exhibiting, via two local gap substitutions
and LTE, phase differences realizing every 3-adic valuation and two incompatible unit ratios, to
invoke the Kneser/Lev/Grynkiewicz dichotomy favorably) is a genuine, substantial, unattempted
number-theoretic construction.** Recorded honestly as neither closed nor resolved: the cheap gate
plausibly survives, but the item's real content remains untested, distinct from items that
collapsed outright.

## Summary of this follow-up pass

Of the 6 items that were "genuinely untested" after round 25's main pass (Codex 2, 3, 7, 8, 9;
Gemini 7), 5 now have a real disposition: item 2 closed (reduces to the already-known bounded
`j*(l)-l` oscillation), item 3 closed (classical positional-determinacy argument), item 7 given a
real direct measurement (no new bound, but a concrete, informative negative signal: the budget
ratio needed for small `H` is roughly double the proven covering ratio), item 8 closed (a direct
consequence of item 7's data), item 9 partially assessed (cheap gate plausibly passes, hard content
untouched). Gemini's item 7 remains not separately pursued, still redundant with H-001's own work.
Item 9 (Gemini)'s deep dive refuted the specific fixed-constant claim while leaving a
dimension-aware, harder version genuinely open. No new bound on `j*(l)` or resolution of GAP A/B/C
resulted from any of this; the honest yield is the same shape as round 25's main pass: real
mechanisms tested, most collapsing or reducing to already-known obstructions, one (item 7's direct
`H_{n,Q}` measurement) producing a new, if negative, quantitative data point worth keeping.

## Codex item 9's actual content attempted (not just its go/no-go gate): a genuine positive result

Item 9's real claim (beyond the cheap additivity gate already reasoned through above) needs
"phase differences at every 3-adic valuation and at least two incompatible unit ratios" to exclude
the "confined to a coset" branch of its Kneser/Lev/Grynkiewicz dichotomy. Codex's own "boundary
state" and "return word" are underspecified, so this used a reasonable, explicit operationalization
rather than a verified match to Codex's precise intent (flagged honestly, not presented as
confirmed): boundary state `sigma := 1 mod 3^q`; a "return word" is a gap-sequence (via `core.py`'s
`G_n=Syrac_n`) whose value returns to `sigma`, extended `n=q+extra` digits for phase room; "phase"
is `discrete_log_4` of the return word's value at level `n`
(`experiments/E-005-round24-checks/round25_item9_codex_phase_cocycle_test.py`).

**Result: q=3, extra=5, budget<=16 gives 193 distinct phases among return words, and pairwise phase
differences realize valuations `v_3 in {2,3,4,5,6}` within this bounded search, with BOTH unit
residues mod 3 (1 and 2) present at EVERY one of these valuations.** This is exactly the two
conditions item 9 asks for, within the tested range: a genuine positive finding, distinct from most
of this round's items, which collapsed or reduced to already-known obstructions. Two honest
caveats: valuations below `q-1` never appeared across the tested `(q,extra)` pairs, looking like a
structural floor tied to the `sigma=1 mod 3^q` return condition rather than a search-budget limit,
not proven either way; and "every valuation" was only checked up to what a bounded budget reaches
(`v_3<=6`), not unboundedly. Whether this actually licenses invoking the Kneser/Lev/Grynkiewicz
dichotomy in item 9's favor (Codex's own next claimed step, real additive-combinatorics machinery)
was not attempted here; that is separate, substantial work, genuinely open.

## Gemini item 9's dimension-aware isoperimetric version: still not attempted, real new geometry needed

Revisited per the researcher's request to look again at everything still open. The earlier deep
dive refuted item 9's claim exactly as stated (a fixed-constant volume threshold). A corrected
version, with a threshold that depends on `Delta_{j,l}`'s actual growing dimension (`~1.1442l+1`)
rather than a plain `3^l*log(3^l)`, remains the one genuinely uninvestigated possibility. This is
not a quick check: it requires deriving (or locating in the literature) the correct
dimension-dependent isoperimetric/concentration constant for this specific combinatorial simplex
and its highly non-Lipschitz pushforward map, not something a bounded computational test can
settle the way Codex's item 9 above could. Left explicitly open, not attempted, rather than forced.

# 2026-07-31 (round 26): a researcher-supplied batch of 56 papers, triaged, and a genuine new
# obstruction proof (free-fermion/determinantal route to S(t), closed rigorously)

The researcher supplied a zip of 56 PDFs (`literature/papers/round26-batch/`), the output of a
broad, distant literature sweep (matching E-004's own search-loop pattern: statistical mechanics,
random matrix theory, probability on trees/graphs, analytic number theory, combinatorics). Triaged
by 4 parallel general-purpose agents (14 files each), each reading title/authors/abstract and, where
ambiguous, the introduction, against this project's actual open questions and its list of already-
exhausted approach categories (given to each agent explicitly, to avoid re-suggesting dead ends).

**Result: 54 of 56 are genuinely irrelevant** (percolation, QFT ladder diagrams, random matrix
spectral universality, empirical processes, automorphic representations, hypergraph Ramsey theory,
stable-distribution special functions, and more; full per-file table with real titles/authors/years
verified by the agents, kept in the agent transcripts, not reproduced here to avoid bloating this
file). Two rated "worth a closer look", both now indexed (L-073, L-074, see literature/INDEX.md).

## L-073 (Okounkov-Reshetikhin) motivated a real structural question, now closed by Codex

Read directly (pp.1-6): exact correlation functions for a random monotone-array (skew plane
partition) model, computed via free-fermion/Schur-process/vertex-operator technology with NO
character-sum cancellation. This prompted a genuine structural observation: the set of admissible
`R_{j,k}` exponent tuples (`alpha_0>...>alpha_j`, each in `{0,...,j+l-1}`) is exactly a free-fermion
occupation-number configuration (choosing `j+1` of `j+l` sites), and the value `sum_i 2^{alpha_i}
3^i` has the shape `(base)^{alpha_i} * (fugacity)^{rank}`, a "staircase" rank-dependent weight of
exactly the kind that appears in classical strict-partition/determinantal generating functions.

**Sent to Codex (gpt-5.6-sol, max reasoning) for a full derivation**, per the researcher's explicit
instruction to consult it on genuinely new ideas. Full prompt/response at
`/home/rat/.claude/jobs/a8d2d60e/tmp/codex_freefermion_{prompt,out}.txt` (key results reproduced and
independently verified below, not taken on faith per Rule 8c). Codex's derivation, real algebraic
work not hand-waving:

1. **`S(t)` is exactly a loop elementary symmetric function** (Lam-Pylyavskyy's colored/"loop"
   extension of symmetric-function theory, arXiv:1012.1262): setting `x_a = exp(2*pi*i*t*2^a/3^l)`,
   `S(t) = sum_{a_1>...>a_m} prod_r x_{a_r}^{3^{r-1}}` is, after reversing indices, the monomial
   quasisymmetric polynomial `M_{(3^{m-1},...,3,1)}(x_0,...,x_{N-1})`. Reversing which color each
   site gets under permutation is exactly what makes it quasisymmetric, not symmetric.
2. **A genuine, explicit LGV (Lindstrom-Gessel-Viennot) determinant exists**: `S(t) = det K`, `K`
   an `m x m` upper-Hessenberg matrix whose entries `H_{r,s}` are themselves weakly-ordered
   multi-sums carrying the SAME rank coupling. **Independently verified numerically** (this
   session, not taken on faith): brute-force `S(t)` at `N=6, m=2` matches the claimed 2x2
   determinant formula exactly (`-1.0499+0.4287j` both ways).
3. **The hoped-for ordinary free-fermion/Vandermonde determinant does NOT exist, proved (not just
   suspected) via an explicit Plucker-relation violation.** If the two-particle amplitudes
   `p_ab = x_a*x_b^3` (a>b) were 2x2 minors of a one-particle orbital matrix (i.e. genuinely
   free-fermionic / Slater-determinant-representable), they would satisfy the Grassmann-Plucker
   relation `p_ab*p_cd - p_ac*p_bd + p_ad*p_bc = 0` for `a>b>c>d`. Substituting gives
   `x_a*x_b^3*x_c*x_d^3`, NOT zero for generic roots of unity `x`. **Independently verified
   numerically** (this session): computed both sides at random `a,b,c,d` and a random `z` on the
   unit circle, exact match, confirmed nonzero
   (`experiments/E-005-round24-checks/round26_freefermion_verification.py`, both checks above).
4. **The structural reason**: the occupation-Hamiltonian `F(n) = sum_a 2^a n_a 3^{R_a}` (`R_a` =
   rank = number of occupied sites above `a`) expands, via `3^{R_a} = prod_{b>a}(1+2n_b)`, into
   interactions of every order. `z^{F(n)}` is not the exponential of a quadratic fermionic
   operator, so Wick's theorem and the standard determinantal-point-process identity do not apply.
   The configuration space is a free-fermion Fock space, but the specific OBSERVABLE being summed
   is a genuinely many-body one. This is the precise, provable reason the "obvious" picture fails.
5. **Broader literature survey, all checked against real citations, all failing for the same
   reason**: loop Schur/alternant formulas (exact match, but the alternant's own entries already
   contain the coupled sums, Frieden arXiv:1504.03782), ordinary LGV/nonintersecting paths (exact
   but circular, per point 2), Schur process/vertex operators (hypotheses don't match, no
   skew-Schur/Toeplitz structure here), Eynard-Mehta (its own normalization determinant IS `S(t)`,
   circular), shifted Schur/Pfaffian process (Vuletic, strictness alone insufficient), RSK/MacMahon
   (fails a cross-ratio/product-form test), free-fermion six-vertex models (Aggarwal-Borodin-
   Petrov-Wheeler, needs Yang-Baxter-compatible rapidities, arbitrary rank-site phases aren't
   covered), TASEP (Schutz's determinants solve a specific Markov evolution, not a static
   potential), dimers/Kasteleyn (fixing a residue sector needs the same coefficient extraction).
   A second concrete test (closure of the WCC specialization under the two-color birational
   R-matrix that generates loop integrability) also fails, with an explicit worked computation.

**Codex's own honest confidence: 3%** that this general direction produces a new bound, with two
already-executed cheap go/no-go tests (the Plucker relation, the R-matrix closure) both failing, no
further computation recommended without first exhibiting a genuinely new algebraic structure
(bounded displacement rank, a subtraction-free factorization with positive unit coefficients, or
similar) that none of the surveyed frameworks currently supply.

**Net effect**: a real, rigorous, independently-verified proof that the free-fermion/determinantal
route to `S(t)` fails, and exactly why (the rank-dependent weight makes the relevant observable
many-body, not free-fermionic). This is a genuinely new result for this project (not previously
derived in any of the prior 25 rounds), even though its content is negative: it closes off an entire
class of techniques (ordinary Slater-determinant/free-fermion methods) that had not been
systematically checked before, with a real algebraic reason rather than an analogy-based guess.

**Citation check (Rule 11), 2026-07-31, before treating this as publication-ready material**: the
researcher asked whether this finding is publishable; per this project's own standard, that
requires the underlying citations to be independently verified against primary sources, not taken
on Codex's word. All 9 of Codex's cited sources checked directly against arXiv, title/author/content
match confirmed for every one, none fabricated or misdescribed:

- Lam, "Loop symmetric functions and factorizing matrix polynomials", arXiv:1012.1262 -- confirmed
  (joint work with Pylyavskyy on the ring of loop symmetric functions, matches).
- Frieden, "A Ratio of Alternants Formula for Loop Schur Functions", arXiv:1504.03782 -- confirmed
  (proves loop Schur functions as a ratio of loop alternants, matches exactly).
- Borodin-Rains, "Eynard-Mehta theorem, Schur process, and their Pfaffian analogs",
  arXiv:math-ph/0409059 -- confirmed.
- Vuletic, "Shifted Schur process and asymptotics of large random strict plane partitions",
  arXiv:math-ph/0702068 -- confirmed (Pfaffian point process for strict partitions, matches exactly).
- Aggarwal-Borodin-Petrov-Wheeler, "Free Fermion Six Vertex Model: Symmetric Functions and Random
  Domino Tilings", arXiv:2109.06718 -- confirmed (free-fermion six-vertex, Yang-Baxter, matches).
- Schutz, "Exact Solution of the Master Equation for the Asymmetric Exclusion Process",
  arXiv:cond-mat/9701019 -- confirmed real (title/author match); the determinant-formula claim
  itself is well-established elsewhere in the TASEP literature, not independently re-derived here.
- Kenyon, "Lectures on Dimers", arXiv:0910.3129 -- confirmed real; the Kasteleyn-determinant content
  is the well-known core of these notes, not independently re-derived here.
- Iwao-Motegi-Ohkawa, "Tetrahedron equation and Schur functions", arXiv:2405.10011 -- confirmed,
  abstract explicitly states partition functions expressed as loop elementary symmetric functions,
  matching Codex's characterization precisely.
- Iwao-Motegi-Ohkawa, "Tetrahedral L-operators, tensor Schur polynomials and q-deformed loop
  elementary symmetric functions", arXiv:2604.22141 (submitted April 2026) -- confirmed, a genuine
  q-deformed extension of loop elementary symmetric functions, matching.

No fabricated or misdescribed citation found. Combined with this session's own independent numerical
verification of the two load-bearing algebraic claims (the LGV determinant, the Plucker-relation
violation), the mathematical content here is now checked to this project's normal standard, not
merely asserted. **Publishability assessment, given directly to the researcher**: the result is a
correct, verified, but narrow negative lemma (one technique class ruled out, via straightforward
application of existing theory to a new object, not new theory itself); not substantial enough for a
standalone paper, but real, citable material for an eventual WCC paper's obstruction survey, and a
genuine addition to this project's own record of what has been tried and why it fails.

**Full-text read of all 9 citations, 2026-07-31 (researcher's explicit request, "did you actually
read them or just check they exist"), not just abstract-level verification**: the 4 most central
papers (Frieden's ratio-of-alternants paper, Lam's foundational loop-symmetric-functions paper, and
both Iwao-Motegi-Ohkawa papers, 2024 and April 2026) read directly by this session; the remaining 5
(Borodin-Rains, Vuletic, Aggarwal-Borodin-Petrov-Wheeler, Schutz, Kenyon) read in full by a
dedicated subagent (WebFetch's own summarizer failed to parse all 5 PDFs and returned boilerplate
non-answers for each; caught, and the raw PDF bytes read directly with the PDF-native tool instead
of trusting that failure silently).

Findings that go beyond the earlier abstract-level check: Frieden's own paper states, as an
explicit open problem, that loop Schur functions "do not span the ring of loop symmetric functions,
and are not even linearly independent" (no known Schur-like basis for LSym_m exists), a genuine
structural limitation beyond what the earlier survey captured, though it does not change the
verdict (Lam's own paper shows the loop ELEMENTARY functions, a different, larger family, ARE
algebraically independent generators of the same ring, Theorem 4.4, so this is a real subtlety, not
a contradiction). Both Iwao-Motegi-Ohkawa papers confirmed, on direct reading, to realize loop
elementary symmetric functions only as one specific construction tied to a particular
integrable-lattice-model setup (Zamolodchikov-Faddeev algebra, tetrahedral L-operators), not a
general evaluation formula. Of the 5 read by the subagent, Kenyon's dimer lectures gave the closest
thing to a hidden lead: extracting a partition function's fixed flux/homology sector on a torus
needs a discrete Fourier average over twisted Kasteleyn determinants (`Z=(1/2)(Z00+Z10+Z01-Z11)`),
and the paper's own Open Problem 1 flags the analogous height-function quantity as unresolved with
"only partial results known." This is not an escape route: it is the SAME coefficient-extraction
difficulty this project is trying to avoid, appearing again in dimer language, which if anything
reinforces the negative verdict rather than undermining it. **No missed formula, evaluation, or
bound was found in any of the 9 papers on full reading.** The negative result stands, now checked
as thoroughly as this project's own standards require before treating a finding as settled.

## L-074 (Taylor-Crush): relevant in principle to the round-25 fugacity-operator item, not applicable as-is

Gives a k-th order perturbative expansion of a random dynamical system's invariant density,
GIVEN that a uniform spectral gap already holds across a perturbation family. Read pp.1-4. This is
exactly the shape of tool that would help H-003's round-25 "microcanonical fugacity transfer
operator" item (Gemini item 6, left genuinely inconclusive: `n=6,7,8` truncations show the spectral
gap shrinking then partially recovering, unable to tell decay from stabilization) -- but only AFTER
a uniform gap is established, which is precisely the unresolved part. Not pursued further; recorded
as a citation worth knowing about if the gap question is ever resolved by other means.

## Round 26 summary

Of 56 supplied papers, 54 confirmed irrelevant after real triage (titles/authors/abstracts checked,
not skipped), 2 read more deeply. One (L-073) motivated a genuinely new investigation, sent to
Codex, and closed with a real, independently-verified proof (not a guess) that free-fermion/
determinantal technology does not bypass the `S(t)` obstruction, for a specific, provable algebraic
reason (the rank-dependent weight is many-body, not free-fermionic). The other (L-074) is a real,
relevant-in-principle technique blocked on an already-known open question, not actionable now. No
new bound on `j*(l)`, but a new class of techniques (free-fermion/determinantal methods) is now
ruled out with a real proof rather than left untried, which is itself useful: it narrows what remains
plausible for anyone (including a future round) attacking the rank-coupling obstruction directly.

`binom(j+k+1,j+1)` times the characteristic function of a finite-population L-statistic with
`3^i`-scored ranks at non-CLT frequencies; GAP C's invariant-density fixed point implies
`X =_d sum_r U_r/3^r` (`U_r` iid Uniform[0,2]), moving it into small-deviations/perpetuity/

## First batch of 8 papers found by the researcher's own manual search (2026-07-31, round27-batch)

Read directly (L-084 through L-091, literature/INDEX.md). Confirms Codex's own A1/A4 citations
exactly (L-085/L-086 are the published versions of arXiv:1204.2109/1103.4220; L-084 matches A4),
and surfaces 4 more papers by the same author (Andrius Ciginas, Vilnius University, 2011-2019: exact
bootstrap variance, second-order approximations, calibrated bootstrap/saddlepoint, calibrated
Edgeworth), all genuinely real and forming a coherent, active research line in finite-population
`L`-statistics, exactly matching GAP A's structural identification (`S(t)` as a characteristic
function of an `L`-statistic sampled without replacement).

**Read closely enough to assess, not just confirm existence**: every one of these papers targets
CLT-adjacent asymptotics (asymptotic normality, one-term Edgeworth corrections, saddlepoint tail
approximations) under conditions on the weight function `J`. None was found to address, or plausibly
extend to, the specific regime `S(t)` needs: an EXPONENTIALLY-growing weight (`2^{alpha_i}`, not a
bounded or polynomial-growth `J`) evaluated at essentially the FULL range of frequencies `t`,
including extreme ones far from the CLT-relevant scale. This is exactly Codex's own hedged caveat
("existing theorems probably do not tolerate `3^i` scores or the required frequencies"), now
CONFIRMED by direct reading of the actual papers rather than left as a prediction. A genuinely
existing, active, real research line, that on inspection does not (yet) supply the needed result.

The other two papers in this batch: L-084 (Rupcic, SU(1,1) lacunary products, real, matches Codex's
A4) is convergence theory for infinite products, not a finite-sum bound, structurally the wrong tool.
L-091 (Zhu, May 2026, Rankin-Selberg coefficients mod prime powers) is real and very recent but a
different area entirely (automorphic L-functions, level of distribution), no connection to any of
GAP A/B/C found; most likely a keyword-overlap false positive from the search itself, not something
Codex named. Net: no new leverage on any gap from this first batch, but real confirmation that
Codex's citation-generation was accurate and the researcher's manual search is finding the right
neighborhood of literature, just not (yet) a paper that solves the actual problem.

like "small," "weights," "exponential").

**Confirmed real, matching Codex's own citations exactly**: Borovkov & Ruzankin 2008 (Siberian
Adv. Math., an ENGLISH TRANSLATION, doi:10.3103/s1055134408030024, not just the Russian original),
Rozovsky 2007 (J. Math. Sciences, doi:10.1007/s10958-007-0518-2), Saavedra-Araya 2024
(arXiv:2411.07418, "Distribution of integers with digit restrictions via Markov chains"),
Magee-Oh-Winter 2016 (arXiv:1601.03705).

**New, real citations not in Codex's original list**, found via OpenAlex full-text search:

- Rozovsky 2020, same title as the 2007 paper ("Small deviation probabilities for sums of
  independent positive random variables"), Vestnik of St. Petersburg University,
  doi:10.21638/spbu01.2020.307. Not yet determined whether this is a genuine update/extension or a
  re-publication; not read beyond the metadata.
- Rozovsky 2021, "On Small Deviation Asymptotics in the L2-Norm for Certain Gaussian Processes",
  Mathematics (MDPI), doi:10.3390/math9060655. Confirms Rozovsky is still actively publishing in
  this area as recently as 2021, but this specific paper is about Gaussian processes in L2-norm,
  not weighted sums of positive variables; not the right sub-area on its title alone.
- Aurzada & Ibragimov 2008, "Small deviations of smooth stationary Gaussian processes" (both the
  Russian original, Teor. Veroyatnost. Primenen., doi:10.4213/tvp2466, and an arXiv English version,
  arXiv:0803.4238). Same caveat: Gaussian processes, not weighted positive-variable sums.
- Nazarov & Petrova 2023, "L2-small ball asymptotics for Gaussian random functions: a survey",
  arXiv:2305.08927. **Read directly (pp.1-3, table of contents + introduction)**: a real, recent,
  comprehensive survey, but confirmed to be in the WRONG sub-area for GAP C. It covers
  `||X||^2_L2 = sum mu_k*xi_k^2` (a QUADRATIC form in Gaussian variables, weighted by covariance
  eigenvalues via the Karhunen-Loeve expansion), not `X = sum a_j*xi_j` (a LINEAR weighted sum of
  positive variables), which is what Wirsching's `X = sum_r U_r/3^r` fixed-point identity actually
  needs. A real, honest negative: this specific survey does not apply, despite superficially
  matching "small ball"/"small deviations" vocabulary.
- Berg & Krüppel 1998, "Cantor Sets and Integral-Functional Equations", Zeitschrift für Analysis
  und ihre Anwendungen, doi:10.4171/zaa/863. A THIRD, earlier Berg-Krüppel paper not surfaced by
  H-006's own earlier search (which found the 2000-vintage `Z. Anal. Anwendungen 19` and
  `Results in Math. 38` papers, but not this 1998 one). Real, not yet read beyond the abstract;
  worth checking if H-006 is ever picked up again, since it appears to be the earliest paper in
  the same series and could contain the base construction the later papers build on.

Added to literature/INDEX.md as L-092 through L-096. **Net assessment**: real, useful confirmation
that the automated open-access search reaches the same neighborhood Codex named, plus one genuinely
new citation for H-006 (Berg-Krüppel 1998) and two new Rozovsky citations for GAP C worth a closer
read if this thread is picked up again. No new leverage on any gap; nothing here resolves the
actual open question, consistent with every other search this project has run.

## Round 27, "analogous framings" consult (2026-07-31): searching for disguised versions of the
## same objects in fields that would never surface under direct-terminology search

At the researcher's explicit request ("comendo pelas beiradas": search adjacent/exotic fields
for someone who solved a piece of this without knowing it connects to Collatz). Full
prompt/response at `/home/rat/.claude/jobs/a8d2d60e/tmp/codex_analog_{prompt,out}.txt`. Codex
(gpt-5.6-sol, max reasoning) was given the three objects (GAP A's `S(t)`, GAP B's `H_{n,Q}`, GAP
C's invariant density `phi`) stripped of all Collatz/WCC vocabulary and asked to identify EXACT or
near-exact structural matches in unrelated fields, rating each honestly rather than reaching for
surface resemblance. Full detail (search phrases, venues, exactness ratings) in the transcript;
key results below, with the most load-bearing claim independently verified before recording.

**GAP C: `phi` is not analogous to but EXACTLY (up to translation) Rvachev's atomic function
`h_3`.** Rvachev's `h_a` (Soviet/Ukrainian tradition, "atomic functions", compactly supported
solutions of `(2/a^2)h_a'(x) = h_a(ax+1) - h_a(ax-1)`) at `a=3` satisfies EXACTLY the same
functional equation as `phi` after centering. **Independently verified**, not taken on Codex's
word: with `psi(y) := phi(y+1/2)`, the already-established equation `phi'(t) = (9/2)(phi(3t) -
phi(3t-2))` (H-006's own citation) gives `psi'(y) = (9/2)(psi(3y+1) - psi(3y-1))`, which is
EXACTLY Rvachev's equation at `a=3`. So `phi(x) = h_3(x - 1/2)` is a genuine identity, not an
analogy. The SAME object was independently rediscovered in Japan: K. Kabaya & M. Iri, "Sum of
uniformly distributed random variables and a family of non-analytic C^infinity-functions", Japan
J. Applied Math. 4 (1987), 1-22 -- a title that literally describes `X = sum_r U_r/3^r` (this
project's own construction of `phi`) without any Collatz/Wirsching framing at all, exactly the
kind of independently-discovered, differently-named result this search round was looking for.
Neither paper is yet confirmed (by primary-source read) to contain the specific near-0 asymptotic
ratio-convergence statement Conjecture 3 needs -- an exact object match is not evidence the
missing theorem exists in that literature, only that it is now findable if it does.

**GAP C methodological lead (separate from the identity match): Mellin harmonic sums / Mahler
partitions / de Bruijn's log-periodic analysis.** Taking logs of `phi`'s Laplace transform gives a
base-3 "harmonic sum" (Flajolet-Gourdon-Dumas terminology) whose Mellin transform generically
produces a LOG-PERIODIC correction, not a constant -- the classical precursor being de Bruijn's
1948 sharpening of Mahler's partition asymptotic. This directly explains why Berg-Kruppel's own
paper only claims a BOUNDED (not convergent) factor: a bounded log-periodic multiplier is exactly
what this machinery predicts in general. Codex derived a specific, checkable mechanism for why
Conjecture 3's SPECIFIC sequence `t_l ~ l*3^{-l}` might still work even though the general ratio
oscillates: saddle-point inversion gives `log_3(s(t_l))` approaching a fixed phase as `l -> inf`
along that particular sequence, i.e. the sequence may "phase-lock" a log-periodic multiplier to a
single value. This is inference from the transform structure, not a proof, and not yet tested.

**GAP A**: no exact vanishing/bounding theorem found, but a real, verified-structure algebraic
home identified: `S(t)` is exactly a one-column Macdonald "ninth-variation" Schur function at a
cyclotomic specialization (entry-and-row-dependent tableau weight, matching the value-and-rank
dependence exactly), and separately exactly an instance of Schlosser's weight-dependent binomial
coefficients (arbitrary two-coordinate step weight in a lattice-path sum). Both give a genuine
algebraic vocabulary but, per Codex's own honest assessment, no known product formula or vanishing
criterion for the SPECIFIC lacunary specialization `w(a,r) = exp(2*pi*i*t*2^a*3^r/3^L)` needed
here; classical `q`-Lucas/cyclotomic root-of-unity criteria for ORDINARY Gaussian binomials do NOT
transfer (checked explicitly: the relevant operators `A_a f(z) = z^{2^a} f(z^3)` do not commute up
to a scalar `q`, so the standard theory's core mechanism is absent).

**GAP B**: the outer orbit `{4^k mod 3^n}` is exactly a multiplicative congruential
pseudorandom-number generator restricted to the principal-unit subgroup (real citation: Mérai &
Shparlinski, arXiv:2302.03964, prime-power PRNG discrepancy), and `H_{n,Q}` is exactly a Lee
covering-radius problem for a nonlinear one-dimensional code -- but per Codex's own assessment,
this is the weakest of the three matches: none of the found literature handles the highly
STRUCTURED (rank-coupled) reachable subset directly, only the full orbit or generic subsets, so
this reformulation does not obviously help beyond what this project's own direct measurement
(round 26/27, `H_{n,Q}` needs ~2x the proven budget ratio) already established. Codex explicitly
rejected the Beatty/three-distance-theorem angle the researcher's own earlier round had also
flagged as unpromising (log/discrete-log transformations destroy the ordinary ordering gaps are
measured in, so three-distance machinery does not transfer here) -- consistent with, not
contradicting, this project's own item-9(Codex)/round-25 conclusions on related questions.

**Not pursued further this session, flagged for a future round if picked up**: fetching and
reading Kabaya-Iri's two 1987/1988 papers and the modern Ukrainian "atomic function" continuation
literature (Brysina, Makarichev et al., already partially surfaced in round 27's GAP C search,
L-096's neighborhood) specifically for a boundary/near-0 asymptotic result; and a direct
computational test of the log-periodic phase-locking mechanism (compute `phi(t_l)/g_0(t_l)` at
several `l` numerically, already flagged as cheap in L-002.md, and check whether it empirically
looks constant vs. oscillating as `l` grows, which the phase-locking argument above would predict
converges even though the general off-sequence ratio need not).

## Round 28, DOI-hunt batch (2026-07-31): H-008's two named leads read in full, plus L-021 and L-090

The researcher tracked down and supplied PDFs for several DOIs this project had recorded as
inaccessible, including the two papers H-008's round-18 brainstorm named for GAP A ("Pascal-Mahler
minor" and "finite-state encoder"), plus L-021 (Anisimov) and L-090 (Ciginas-Pumputis), previously
only abstract-level.

**Baker, "p-Adic Continuous Functions on Rings of Integers and a Theorem of K. Mahler," J. London
Math. Soc. 33 (1986), 414-420 (L-060), read in full (7pp).** Confirms this is purely classical
basis theory: it generalizes Mahler's binomial-coefficient basis for `C(Z_p, Z_p)` to a basis
(via Teichmuller characters and a recursively-defined polynomial sequence `theta_k`) for
continuous functions on the ring of integers of any finite extension `E/Q_p`. No exponential-sum
bounding technique, no vanishing/non-vanishing criterion at roots of unity, nothing that attacks
`S(t)` directly. Confirms, by full read rather than abstract-guess, that the "Pascal-Mahler
triangularization" direction has no ready-made tool in this specific paper.

**Adler, Friedman, Kitchens & Marcus, "State Splitting for Variable-Length Graphs," IEEE Trans.
Inform. Theory IT-32(1) (1986), 108-113 (L-061), read in full (6pp).** Confirms this is a real,
complete, applicable algorithm (Main Theorem/Theorem 5): given a variable-length constrained graph
`G` and a target rate `n`, with `cap(G) >= log_2(n)`, a sequence of state-splittings produces an
`n`-codable subgraph, from which a rate-1:1 finite-state encoder can be built. This is a genuine
construction tool for H-008's "finite-state encoder" direction, not just a citation-level
precedent. It is a CONSTRUCTION method starting from a known graph and its transition-matrix
capacity (computed via `det(A(z)-I)=0`, Shannon's formula), not a bound-proving technique on its
own. Applying it to `R_{j,k}` would require first formalizing the admissible-tuple structure
(strictly-decreasing exponent sequences) as a variable-length graph and computing its capacity --
nontrivial translation work, not attempted this round, and not obviously easier than the direct
approaches already tried in 27 prior rounds.

**Anisimov, "Integer representation in the mixed base (2,3)," Cybernetics and Systems Analysis 45
(2009), 503-516 (L-021), read in full (14pp).** Previously flagged as "the structurally closest
match found... to R_{j,k}'s own mixed-base construction" on abstract level alone; **that
characterization does not survive a full read**. The (2,3)-decomposition is `x = 2^n1 +
3^k1(2^n2 + 3^k2(2^n3 + ...))`, where the 3-exponents are free-choice positive-integer "block
weights" `k_i >= 1` (subject to structural theorems about their distribution and "critical
blocks"), not the fixed, consecutive integer index `i = 0, 1, ..., j` that `R_{j,k}`'s own
construction requires (`sum_i 2^alpha_i * 3^i`). The paper is entirely about bijective,
error-tolerant prefix coding of a single arbitrary integer (Theorems 1-6, two competing codes
C+/C, modular div-by-3 algorithms for hardware), with zero covering/surjectivity-mod-3^l content.
Confirmed structural mismatch, same conclusion pattern as L-095/L-106.

**Ciginas & Pumputis, "Calibrated Edgeworth Expansions of Finite Population L-Statistics" (2019,
L-090), read in full (methodology sections, ~8pp).** Confirms this is applied-statistics
ESTIMATION methodology (jackknife, bootstrap, calibration via Deville-Sarndal) for estimating the
unknown Edgeworth-expansion parameters `alpha, kappa` of a Studentized L-statistic from sample
data with auxiliary variables. GAP A needs a bound on a fully explicit, already-known weighted sum
`S(t)`, not an estimator for unknown population parameters. Confirmed wrong tool, same conclusion
as the earlier abstract-level read, now on a full read.

**Net assessment**: four more real, correctly-identified papers, all now read in full rather than
guessed from title/abstract, all confirmed not to supply what GAP A or the H-008 sub-leads need.
No new leverage; H-008 stays backlog, H-003's core obstruction remains open. This does close out
the "read the two H-008 leads in full" action item cleanly, with an honest negative rather than an
untested guess.

derived, verified three ways, and recorded as "a minor but real result: the first proof that e(l)

set.** Codex proposed reducing `F_{N,m}(X) := sum_{N>a_0>...>a_{m-1}>=0} X^{sum_i 2^{a_i}3^i}`
(the generating polynomial for `S(t) = F_{N,m}(zeta_{3^L}^t)`) mod 3, exploiting that `(1+u)^3 =
1+u^3 mod 3` (Frobenius) matches this project's own `X -> X^3` rank-coupling step exactly, and

for any `t` coprime to 3 forces `u^{2*3^{L-1}} | G_{N,m}(u) mod 3` (`G_{N,m}(u) := F_{N,m}(1+u)
mod 3`). **Independently verified, not taken on faith** (`experiments/E-007-frobenius-mod3-
valuation/`): (a) brute-force-checked Codex's claimed recursion `F_{N,m}(X) = F_{N-1,m}(X) +
X^{2^{N-1}}*F_{N-1,m-1}(X^3)` against direct enumeration, N=1..6, exact match every case; (b)

`m=j`) for `j=1..30` (`l` up to ~27-38 via `j~log_4(3)*l`, past this project's current computed

tested, for the `R_{j-1,j}` family -- a genuine, checkable, new-to-this-project result (25+ prior
rounds never found this specific Frobenius-mod-3 reduction). **Honest scope, matching Codex's own

## Round 30 (2026-07-31): a second, longer (up to 10-round) "where are we stuck" cycle

See `notes/H-006.md` for round 1's H-006/Conjecture 3 content (a candidate rigorous proof sketch,
