**Round 4: a concrete worked example, verified exactly, and a genuine self-correction that
downgrades the pressure-gap route's practicality.** Asked Codex to propose its own precise local
update rule for the block transfer-matrix idea and work a `B=3` example. Gave the exact recursion
`C_{a+1,r} = C_{a,r} UNION (2^a + 3*C_{a,r-1}) mod M` (`C_{a,r}` = residues after inspecting
exponents `0..a-1`, selecting exactly `r`), correctly identified as exact with `C_{2j,j} =
R_{j-1,j} mod 3^L`, and computed by hand: `R_{2,3} mod 27 = {2,4,8,10,11,17,19,20,22,23,26}`
(11 distinct residues from `C(6,3)=20` tuples).

**Independently verified by direct brute-force enumeration: exact match**, both the tuple count
and the full residue set, digit for digit.

**Then Codex caught a real problem in its own round-1/round-3 transfer-matrix sketch**: the
naive `2^B`-state model (a local `B`-bit "which positions selected" mask) is genuinely
insufficient, because it "loses the residue `y mod 3^L`," "loses the selected-count grading," and
"`2^a mod 3^L` makes the block transfer layer-dependent" -- concretely, residues `y=0` and `y=3`
mod 27 cannot be merged into the same local state, since a further selection maps them to
different final residues (`2^a` vs `2^a+9`). **This means the actual state space needed is the
full residue set mod `3^L` (exponentially large in `L`, the same scale as the original problem),
not the small local mask (`2^B` to `4^B` states) the earlier state-size table assumed.** The
independent-sector identity (`rho(Q_ind) = rho(T)^2` for uncorrelated block fillings) is confirmed
sound on its own terms, but "an 8-state numerical pressure matrix requires an additional, proved
quotient/relaxation from the exact residue transfer... I do not currently see such a quotient
preserving coverage."

**Net assessment**: a real, honest correction, not a minor caveat. The pressure-gap route, as
concretely specified, does not currently offer the computational tractability its earlier framing
suggested -- the state space collapses back to the same order as the original covering problem
unless a genuinely new state-compression idea is found. Not closed outright (Codex still holds
some confidence in the general transfer-operator framing), but materially downgraded from round
1's "testable with blocks of size 6,8,10." Queued for round 5: ask whether any compression is
possible, or treat this specific concretization as exhausted and ask for a different idea.

**Round 5: the coarse-modulus compression idea explicitly closed, by Codex's own correct
reasoning; one narrower idea remains.** Asked directly whether any state compression exists.
Codex closed the natural "track only mod `3^ell`, `ell<L`" idea itself: coarse coverage of a
residue class mod `3^ell` gives NO nontrivial bound on how many of its `3^{L-ell}` fine lifts are
actually hit (could be 1, could be all), so "unless the transitions admit a genuine digit-
separation/Hensel-lift statement, one must retain the full fibre distribution. Nothing presently
suggests that separation here." Real, checkable reasoning, not a hedge.

**One remaining concrete test proposed**: track the representation-COUNT function `mu(x)`
(not Boolean support) and group characters `t` by their 3-adic conductor `3^c`. If
`M_c := max_{cond(t)=3^c} |mu_hat(t)|/mu_hat(0)`, the sufficient criterion
`sum_{c=1}^{L} phi(3^c)*M_c < 1` implies full coverage. This is `O(L)`-scale IF the conductor-
grouped bound can be controlled uniformly, not character-by-character (still `O(3^L)`
individually). Codex's own explicit caveat: "a stronger sufficient condition, not an equivalent
formulation: failure would not refute coverage," and its own stopping criterion: "If the
conductor envelopes do not close ... I would regard GAP A as genuinely exhausted for this
project." Not yet attempted; this is essentially a coarser-grained version of the original
S(t)-magnitude question this whole investigation started from, now reframed by conductor level
rather than individual character -- worth one real attempt before treating GAP A as exhausted.

**Round 6: GAP A's conductor-Fourier audit closed, by Codex's own concrete counterexample,
independently verified.** Asked whether the conductor-class maximum `M_c` has any closed form or
shortcut. Codex derived the exact per-character recursion (real, and consistent with this
project's own `F_{N,m}` recursion from earlier rounds), but showed the MAXIMUM over a conductor
class does not inherit it (only a lossy triangle-inequality bound), and gave a concrete
counterexample: `F_{4,2}(X) = X^5+X^7+X^10+X^11+X^14+X^20`, with squared moduli at the three
primitive 9th roots of unity (`a=1,2,4`) approximately `1.241, 8.064, 5.695` -- wildly unequal,
showing conductor 9 has no representative-character reduction, not even under the `a -> 2a`
symmetry that cycles the primitive classes.

**Independently verified, exactly**: brute-force-confirmed `F_{4,2}`'s exponent set matches the
actual `R_{1,2}` construction, and recomputed the three squared moduli directly: `1.2412, 8.0642,
5.6946`, matching Codex's claim to 4 decimal places.

**Codex's own conclusion, and this project's own assessment agrees**: "the conductor-max
criterion is not a computational compression: it replaces the direct Fourier `L^1` sum by a
weaker bound requiring essentially the same sweep... this front has reached its natural
theoretical limit for now." **GAP A is now closed across every concrete route attempted in this
consultation cycle**: the Frobenius-mod-3 reduction (real, but proves only non-vanishing, not
magnitude, round 29), the pressure-gap transfer operator (state space collapses to the size of
the original problem, round 4), the coarse-modulus compression (no nontrivial fine-lift bound,
round 5), and now the conductor-Fourier audit (no compression over the direct sweep, round 6).
No further concrete GAP A lead is currently on the table from this cycle.

## Round 31 (2026-07-31): a third consultation cycle, deliberately steered away from GAP A's
## closed frameworks and H-006's overlap-sector thread, asking for genuinely lateral ideas
## (other fields, invented formalisms, hidden numerical patterns), planned up to 10 rounds

Prompt explicitly listed every closed GAP A route (Bajnok, polynomial method, character sums,
coupon-collector/discrepancy, 3-adic transfer operators, free-fermion/determinantal,
Frobenius-mod-3, pressure-gap operator, coarse-modulus and conductor-Fourier compressions,
noncommutative valuation-stratified transfer matrix) and H-006's local overlap-sector model, and
asked Codex not to re-propose any of them; asked instead for cross-field structural matches
(statistical physics, information theory, coding theory, random matrix theory, ...), a possible
invented formalism specific to this problem, a close look at the rank-value coupling
(position `i` times exponent `alpha_i`) as an object in its own right, and any hidden pattern in
the numbers not visible through the frameworks already tried.

**Round 1 response, independently verified before recording anything, per Rule 8c:**

**A genuinely new, confirmed exact reformulation: the Young-diagram / partition rewrite of
`F_j`.** With `lambda_i := alpha_i - (j-1-i)` (the standard bijection between a strictly
decreasing `j`-tuple `alpha_0 > ... > alpha_{j-1} >= 0` bounded by `2j-1`, and a partition
`lambda` fitting in the `j x j` square), Codex derived

```
F_j(lambda) := sum_{i=0}^{j-1} 2^{alpha_i} 3^i = (3^j - 2^j) + sum_{(i,c) in D(lambda)} 3^i * 2^{j-1-i+c}
```

where `D(lambda)` is `lambda`'s Young diagram. **Verified independently two ways**: (a) hand
re-derivation from `2^{alpha_i} = 2^{j-1-i}(1 + sum_{c=0}^{lambda_i-1} 2^c)` and the geometric-sum
identity `sum_i 3^i 2^{j-1-i} = 3^j - 2^j`, both exact; (b) direct numerical check, 200 random
trials with `j` up to 12, comparing the formula against brute-force `F_j` from the actual
`alpha_i`, all match exactly (`verify_round1_lateral.py`). Also verified the accompanying
box-weight facts `v_3(w_{i,c}) = i` and `w_{i+a,c+a} = 3^a * w_{i,c}` (`w_{i,c} := 3^i 2^{j-1-i+c}`
is the value of a single box), 500 random trials, all match. **This is a real, confirmed new exact
reformulation of the covering question**: does the weighted order ideal `D(lambda) subset [j] x [j]`,
under box weights `w_{i,c}`, realize every 3-adic unit after the fixed shift `3^j - 2^j`?

**Speculative extensions, not yet confirmed either way:**

- **A "ternary Hensel encoder" on this lattice**: a finite gadget (a framed family of boundary
  moves over a window, not one exponent per digit) realizing all three lifts
  `C, C+3^r u, C+2*3^r u (mod 3^{r+1})` from a reusable frontier state, which would give a direct
  digit-by-digit right inverse (a constructive covering proof, no Fourier estimate needed).
  Codex's own confidence: 35% it becomes a useful proof route, 90% it is at least the right
  formal object. **A real tension flagged back to Codex, not yet resolved**: an earlier,
  independently verified result (this project's own single-exponent-step "next-digit rule",
  `sigma_r(P) + (-1)^{a_r} (mod 3)`, l=3..7, 300 random tuples) proves that a FIXED prefix can
  only ever realize two of the three possible next digits, never `sigma_r` itself, at the
  single-step level. Whether Codex's block-level gadget genuinely escapes this or just
  reproduces the same wall at a coarser grain is the open question sent back for round 2.
- **A Macdonald/Ruijsenaars-eigenvalue identification**: `F_j(lambda) = 3^{j-1} sum_i 2^{lambda_i}
  (2/3)^{j-1-i}`, algebraically the same identity rewritten (independently re-derived by hand,
  matches), claimed to equal the first Macdonald difference operator's eigenvalue
  `sum_i q^{lambda_i} tau^{n-i}` at `q=2, tau=2/3`. The rewrite itself is confirmed; **the cited
  Macdonald-theory fact (the eigenvalue formula) has NOT been independently checked against a
  primary source this round** and is being treated as plausible-but-unverified, not confirmed,
  pending either a primary-source check or Codex pointing to the exact theorem. Codex's own
  actionable-probability estimate for this route producing an actual covering theorem: 15%.
- **Poset/coding-theory ("weighted ideal-syndrome code") and statistical-mechanics
  (height-dependent exclusion model, `q`-TASEP analogy) framings**: both explicitly flagged by
  Codex itself as language/diagnostic framings rather than proof routes ("I do not claim Bethe
  solvability"; "mainly a proof-certificate framework, not an LP-bound route"). Sent back asking
  whether either makes a concrete, new, checkable prediction beyond what the Young-diagram
  identity already gives, or should be dropped for the rest of this cycle.
- **A multifractal fiber-spectrum heuristic**: `M_{j,l} = C(2j,j) / (2*3^{l-1})`, claimed
  `~ 4^e/sqrt(l)` at `j = l*log_4(3)+e` (a correct Stirling-based asymptotic, hand-checked). Fiber
  counts and hole counts gathered for `l=2..8` by brute force over every `C(2j,j)` tuple
  (`verify_round1_lateral.py`): holes at `j*(l)-1` are `1,1,3,1,3,9,22` for `l=2..8`, matching a
  "tied worst-case residues" count already on record from an earlier round, a good internal
  consistency check. Flagged back to Codex: the "mean fiber size" computed this way is
  definitionally equal to `M_{j,l}` (both are `total_tuples/units_mod`), so this data does not
  independently test the asymptotic claim; asked for a genuinely distinguishing quantity to
  compute instead.

Full j*(l), e(l) table (l=1..23) and the fiber/hole data table sent back to Codex for round 2,
along with the resolved/unresolved status of each item above.

**Round 2 response, independently verified before recording:**

**The Hensel-encoder tension resolved with a real, confirmed local mechanism: a window-2
"revise-then-append" gadget.** Codex's own correction: a literally fixed prefix can never give
all 3 lifts (round 1's obstruction confirmed, unchanged), but allowing one bounded backtrack on
the immediately preceding exponent can. Precisely: with `a := a_{r-1}` fixed, choose a legal next
exponent `b < a`, and require `a_{r-2} > a+4`; for `d=0,1,2` use `P_d = (a_0,...,a_{r-2}, a+2d, b)`.
Then `V_{r+1}(P_d) - V_r(P) = 3^{r-1} 2^a (4^d-1) + 3^r 2^b ≡ 3^r(2^a d + 2^b) (mod 3^{r+1})`,
using `4^d ≡ 1+3d (mod 9)` for `d=0,1,2`; since `2^a` is a unit mod 3, `d=0,1,2` realizes all
three digit lifts. **Verified two ways**: hand re-derivation (exact match), and 50 random numeric
trials (random `r,a,b` and a random valid strictly-decreasing prefix), confirming both the closed
form and that all 3 lifts are genuinely realized every time
(`verify_round2_lateral.py`). This does not solve the global encoder (frontier-state growth is
still the open risk Codex itself flagged), but it is now a real, confirmed local escape from the
single-step 2-of-3 wall, not hand-waving.

**Macdonald citation independently verified against the primary source.** Codex cited Macdonald,
*Symmetric Functions and Hall Polynomials* (2nd ed.), Ch. VI §4, pp. 324-325, formula (4.15). We
fetched the actual text (via `WebFetch` + direct PDF page read) and confirmed it verbatim: formula
(4.15) is `D_n(X;q,t)P_lambda = prod_{i=1}^n (1+Xq^{lambda_i}t^{n-i}) P_lambda`, and the proof of
the adjacent (4.17) explicitly states the first difference operator's eigenvalue as
`sum_i q^{lambda_i} t^{n-i}`. Page, section, and formula number are all correct: this is now a
confirmed real citation, not a plausible-sounding one, per Rule 11. Codex itself does not see a
bridge from this spectral identification to the finite-rectangle fiber minima that would actually
matter for coverage; that gap remains open.

**Fiber-collision statistics, l=2..8 (own bug caught before sending anything to Codex)**: the
first attempt at the requested lift-mask distribution `L_{j,l}(m)` double-counted a trivial case
(a parent residue's unit-ness mod 3 determines all 3 of its lifts' unit-ness at once, since
`x mod 3 = xbar mod 3` independent of `u`; F_j never produces a non-unit value at all, verified by
assertion against every fiber-count table). Corrected to restrict to unit parent classes only.
Two real findings from the corrected data: (a) the collision statistic
`kappa_{j,l} = U/(N(N-1)) * sum_x n_x(n_x-1)` grows steadily above the uniform-random value of 1,
from 1.23 at l=2 to 2.40 at l=8, i.e. real fiber clustering, not a uniform random map, though
whether this growth continues or saturates at larger l is unknown from this range; (b) `L(0)=0`
at every tested l=2..8, meaning no unit parent class mod `3^{l-1}` is ever left with zero live
children among its 3 unit lifts at `j*(l)-1` -- every missing residue has at least one live
sibling, leaning against a "deterministic frozen sector" picture in this range. Flagged to Codex,
per this project's own repeated experience, as suggestive at small l but not conclusive (shallow-
regime patterns have failed to survive to larger l at least twice before in this project).

Sent back for round 3: whether the window-2 gadget composes into an actual bounded-frontier
transducer, and whether the p-adic Hensel-triple-packing idea Codex proposed at the end of round 2
(pack tuples into disjoint triples realizing all 3 lifts mod `3^s` from one common residue mod
`3^{s-1}`, bound the unpacked leftover `B_s`, get a genuine Fourier-cancellation certificate that
could directly close GAP A) is concrete enough yet to test computationally.

**Round 3 response, independently verified before recording:**

**A real, fully confirmed bounded-frontier transducer.** Generalizing the window-2 gadget:
for `P` with last exponent `a` and second-to-last `c` satisfying `c-a>=5`, define three children
`P_d = (..., c, a+2d, a-5+2d)` for `d=0,1,2`. Every child ends with frontier gap exactly `5`
(constant, so the construction is self-similar/recursive), and `F_{r+1}(P_d) - F_r(P) ≡
3^r 2^a (d-1) (mod 3^{r+1})`, hitting all three digit lifts since `2^a` is a unit mod 3. Codex
supplied its own `check(depth)` function asserting this at every recursive level plus a final
check that the leaves' values exactly cover the claimed residue class mod `3^depth`. **Run
verbatim (Codex's own code, unmodified), depth=2 through depth=10: every assertion passed at
every depth**, confirming a genuine two-state (parity-of-last-exponent) transducer that
constructively proves unbounded-depth unit coverage. Our own independent hand/numeric
re-derivation of the closed form initially disagreed (50 mismatches), traced to an off-by-one bug
in our OWN test harness's prefix construction (building `P` with `r+1` elements instead of `r`),
not in Codex's claim; fixed, then 50 trials all matched. **Real caveat, stated by Codex itself and
confirmed by us**: this construction costs 5 exponent levels per ternary digit lifted, so it
proves coverage exists in principle but says nothing about the *rate* (`j*(l)`'s actual growth);
it does not by itself touch the open question.

**A much faster, independently cross-validated transfer-matrix DP for fiber counts.** Codex
supplied a numpy DP (`fiber_counts`, rolling `np.roll` shifts over exponent scan, `O(j*3^l)`
instead of enumerating `C(2j,j)` tuples). Checked exactly against the trusted brute-force fiber
counts for l=2..8: every single one of the `3^l` residues matches, not just aggregate stats.
Confirmed identical. This let us extend the round-2 statistics two levels further than brute force
allowed: at l=9, l=10 (`j*(l)-1`), `kappa` keeps climbing (2.4911, 2.6400) and `L(0)` stays exactly
`0`, i.e. the "no isolated dead parent class" pattern survives past l=8, though still small-l by
this project's usual standards for judging whether a pattern is real or a shallow-regime artifact.

**A decisive negative result: the naive triple-packing feasibility bound fails badly, and gets
worse with l.** Codex's own proposed test (`B_s^opt = sum` over parent classes of the unavoidable
leftover after the best possible unconstrained triple-packing) was computed using the now-verified
DP: `B_opt/U` grows from `~8.2x` at l=3 to `~160.6x` at l=10 (not shrinking, not stabilizing,
accelerating: the l=9-to-10 jump alone is `~4.4x` on the ratio). By Codex's own stated criterion
("if even `B_s^opt` is too large, no triple-packing argument based solely on leftover cardinality
can close the gap"), this strongly suggests the naive/arbitrary-packing version of the idea does
not work; a locality-constrained real matching can only do worse than this arbitrary upper bound
on packing quality, never better. Sent back to Codex for round 4: does this kill the route
outright, is there a non-cardinality-based version that could survive, and is `B_opt`'s growth
possibly just the same non-uniformity signal `kappa` is already tracking (one piece of bad news,
not two)? The rectangle-coverage test Codex also proposed this round (restrict transducer leaves
by minimum last-exponent, test coverage at `O(j*3^{ell-1})` cost) remains unattempted.

**Round 4 response, independently checked, and a decisive follow-up test run ourselves:**

**Triple-packing confirmed closed, by Codex's own words**: "Close the naive triple-packing
branch. Its required accounting condition is decisively false." Codex also gave a real
clarification of the `B_opt/U` growth: writing `s_y = sum_r c_{y,r}` and `d_y = 1-3 min_r(c_{y,r}/s_y)`,
`B_opt = sum_y s_y d_y`, and `B_opt/U = (B_opt/N)(N/U)`; the l=9-to-10 jump is driven mostly by
`N/U` (raw domain/codomain multiplicity, expected to grow given `e(l)>0`), not by `B_opt/N`
(normalized per-tuple imbalance), which actually falls slightly (0.1754 -> 0.1576). Sanity-checked
this decomposition arithmetically (it is a tautological identity, `B_opt/U=(B_opt/N)(N/U)`,
confirmed) and accepted Codex's reading. Not independent evidence the fiber non-uniformity is
itself worsening, but independently fatal to the packing closure regardless.

**We then actually ran the rectangle-coverage test ourselves (Codex had only proposed it, not
run it) rather than waiting for round 5, since it was cheap and decisive**
(`verify_round4_rectangle.py`). For every real `(l,j)` pair (`j=j*(l)` and `j*(l)-1`, l=2..8),
exhaustively searched every root `A` in `[1,2j-1]` and every one of the `3^{l-1}` digit sequences
of the two-state gadget, kept leaves with enough tail room (`last exponent >= j-l`), and measured
what fraction of true unit residues mod `3^l` this specific gadget family reaches:

```
l   j    2j-1  units_mod  achieved   frac    (which j)
2   4       7          6         1   0.167   j*(l)
2   3       5          6         0   0.000   j*(l)-1
3   6      11         18         4   0.222   j*(l)
3   5       9         18         2   0.111   j*(l)-1
4   7      13         54         8   0.148   j*(l)
4   6      11         54         5   0.093   j*(l)-1
5   9      17        162        30   0.185   j*(l)
5   8      15        162        20   0.123   j*(l)-1
6  10      19        486        73   0.150   j*(l)
6   9      17        486        42   0.086   j*(l)-1
7  11      21       1458       158   0.108   j*(l)
7  10      19       1458       107   0.073   j*(l)-1
8  12      23       4374       396   0.091   j*(l)
8  11      21       4374       228   0.052   j*(l)-1
```

**A real, decisive negative result**: coverage caps out around 15-22% at `j*(l)` for small `l`
and trends down as `l` grows (16.7%, 22.2%, 14.8%, 18.5%, 15.0%, 10.8%, 9.1%), with `j*(l)-1`
lower still. This matches the resource-accounting intuition from rounds 3-4 directly: the gadget
spends up to 5 exponent-levels (worst case) or 3 (average over `d=0,1,2`) per ternary digit it
produces, against a real budget of roughly `1.14` exponent-levels per digit (`j*(l) ~ 1.1442*l`,
the mean-payoff-game bound). It is structurally too expensive by close to a factor of 3, and this
shows up directly as most targets being unreachable within the real rectangle, not as a subtler
asymptotic effect. **Closing the whole thread that started at round 1's Hensel-encoder idea**
(Hensel encoder -> window-2 gadget -> two-state transducer -> triple-packing -> rectangle
coverage): every individual piece was genuine, verified mathematics (the transducer really does
prove unbounded-depth coverage in the abstract), but none of it closes GAP A or bears on `j*(l)`'s
rate, because the mechanism is too resource-expensive for the tight budget this problem actually
has. Sent back to Codex for round 5 with an explicit request to go properly lateral again, into a
field genuinely untouched this cycle.

**Round 5 response: Codex agreed the transducer thread is closed, then proposed something we
caught as likely a repeat before running anything.** Codex's new proposal: analyze
`Z_{l,j}(chi) = sum_{lambda in D_j} chi(F_l(lambda))`, grouped by conductor, via a "finite p-adic
skew-product/transfer operator" built from Young-lattice growth rules, with a pressure-threshold
sufficient condition `Delta(c,theta) > theta*log(3)`.

**Caught before spending a round on it, no new computation needed**: round 1's Young-diagram
identity (already verified, 200 trials, exact) is a bijection `lambda_i = alpha_i-(j-1-i)` with
`F_j(lambda) = F_j(alpha)` (same value, not approximate). So `D_j` is just the original tuple
domain reindexed, and `Z_{l,j}(chi) = sum_alpha chi(F_l(alpha)) = S(t)` for
`chi(x)=e(2*pi*i*t*x/3^l)`: exactly this project's original `S(t)` object, not a new quantity.
"Conductor-grouped character sums via a transfer operator with a pressure-gap criterion" is, close
to verbatim, what an EARLIER consultation cycle already ran and closed on this exact object
(3-adic transfer operator diagonalizing to `S(t)` with no compression; a separate pressure-gap
transfer operator whose state space collapsed back to the original problem's size; a
conductor-Fourier audit giving no real compression, with an explicit counterexample) -- all three
listed explicitly in this cycle's own round-1 prompt as routes not to re-propose. Sent back to
Codex for round 6: either explain concretely how the "Young-lattice growth" operator's state space
differs mechanically from the already-collapsed pressure-gap operator, or drop it and give
something that doesn't reduce to `S(t)`/character analysis in any form, honestly, rather than
build code for a route already known to fail.

**Round 6: Codex conceded round 5 cleanly** ("You're right: round 5 was the same closed route,
restated... I cannot point to a concrete state variable that avoids that blow-up. That proposal
should be discarded."), then proposed something genuinely different: a **3-adic lifting
certificate**, no characters or transfer operators at all. Core fact: with `q_l := 2*3^{l-1}`,
`2^{a+k*q_l} ≡ 2^a + k*2^a*3^l (mod 3^{l+1})` for `k=0,1,2`, since `2^{q_l} ≡ 1+3^l (mod
3^{l+1})`. Since `2^a` is a unit mod 3, varying `k=0,1,2` produces all three lifts of one residue
mod `3^l` to mod `3^{l+1}`. The proposed proof target: find, for every residue, a representative
tuple with one "free" exponent `a` separated enough from its neighbors that `a, a+q_l, a+2*q_l`
all stay in the valid tuple domain; induction would need a "buffered cover" invariant (a reserved
slack window that reconstitutes itself level to level).

**The base congruence checked and confirmed real** (`2^{q_l} ≡ 1+3^l (mod 3^{l+1})`, direct
computation, l=1..8, exact match every time; consistent with 2 being a primitive root mod `3^l`
and a lifting-the-exponent-style valuation fact). **But the mechanism as literally stated cannot
fit this problem's actual resource envelope, by orders of magnitude**: `q_l` grows like `3^l`
(exponential in `l`), while the real available exponent range `2j-1` at `j=j*(l)` grows only like
`2.29*l` (linear, since `j*(l) ~ 1.1442*l`). Checked directly: `q_l <= 2j-1` fails already at
`l=1` and (barely) holds only at `l=2`; by `l=10`, `q_l=39366` is roughly 1400x the entire
available budget (`29`). Unlike the earlier transducer's "real but ~3x too expensive on average"
problem, this is an exponential-vs-linear mismatch with no rescue by a constant-factor
improvement. Sent back to Codex for round 7: is there a way to use the real congruence with a
smaller effective shift or a between-tuple application that stays within a linear budget, or is
the fact simply the wrong tool for this regime; also asked whether three real-but-wrong-resource-
regime results in a row (window gadget, transducer, now `q_l`-shift) points to a structural reason
every local/constructive lifting mechanism found so far is budget-inefficient here.

**Round 7: Codex proved the `q_l`-shift mechanism is dead outright (not just impractical), via a
real group-theory argument, then proposed one further, more general local-certificate class.**
Core lemma: if `2^d ≡ 1 (mod 3^l)`, since `ord_{3^l}(2) = q_l` (2 is a primitive root mod `3^l`),
group theory forces `q_l | d`; any literal "one exponent returns to the same power-of-2 value mod
`3^l`" mechanism therefore needs an exponent difference of at least `2*q_l`, a hard lower bound,
not just an inconvenient formula. **Independently verified the underlying fact from scratch**:
computed `ord_{3^l}(2)` directly (not just checking the claimed congruence held) for `l=1..7`,
exactly equal to `q_l = 2*3^{l-1}` every time, confirming 2 really is a primitive root mod `3^l`
in this range. Codex named this the "pro-3 orbit-return barrier" and extended the claim to cover
window shifts and the earlier transducer family too, as one unified phenomenon.

**Caught a likely overreach in that extension, sent back rather than accepted at face value**: the
window-2/transducer mechanism (rounds 2-4) does not work via any single exponent's power-of-2
orbit returning to itself; it works via two DIFFERENT positions' weighted contributions summing
so the lower digits are automatically unchanged (a trivial position-weight fact), with the next
digit controlled by a small additive parameter, not a `q_l`-scale return. Its real measured cost
was a ~3x constant-factor overshoot (rounds 3-4), not exponential, which is hard to reconcile with
being "the same barrier" as the q_l-shift's genuinely exponential wall. Asked Codex to defend or
retract the generalization.

**A first cheap computational probe of Codex's proposed remaining route ("whole-tuple additive
cancellation," pairs/triples of full tuples with unaligned support agreeing mod `3^l`)**: targeted
search for the cheapest possible case (two real `R_{j-1,j}` tuples differing by a single swapped
element, i.e. symmetric difference exactly 2, landing in the same residue mod `3^l`) found a real
example immediately at every `l=2..5` tested (`verify_round7_lateral.py`), e.g. `l=5`:
`{0,...,8}` and `{1,...,8,10}` both give `217 (mod 243)`. Cheap same-fiber pairs are not rare. This
only tests "same residue mod `3^l`", not yet the actual target (a controlled 0/`3^l`/`2*3^l` split
mod `3^{l+1}` with small support); sent back to Codex to specify the precise search before
committing more compute.

**Round 8: Codex retracted the overreach cleanly, AND caught a real flaw in our own round-7
probe.** On the overreach: "You're right: I retract the broad 'unified orbit-return barrier'
claim... I have no valid reduction from that mechanism to `q_l`" -- confirmed the transducer's
positional-cancellation mechanism is genuinely different from, and not bound by, the `q_l` orbit
lemma. **On our probe**: Codex showed our four "cheap same-fiber pairs" were all the exact same
trivial family, `A_j=(0,...,j-1)`, `B_j=(1,...,j-1,j+1)`, with `P_j(B_j;2)-P_j(A_j;2) = 3^j`
exactly; since `j=j*(l) > l` always, this difference vanishes mod `3^{l+1}` too, carrying zero
real next-digit information. **Independently confirmed this diagnosis is correct** by inspecting
our own found examples against the stated family (exact match on both index patterns). A real,
useful catch: the round-7 "positive signal" was a false positive, an easy trap (any
consecutive-integer tuple admits this trivial swap), not a preliminary finding as first read.

Codex specified the correct test: the lift mask `M_l(r) = {(P(A;2)-r)/3^l mod 3 : tuple A with
P(A;2)=r mod 3^l}`, evaluated at the REAL covering budget `j=j*(l)` (not `j*(l)-1` as rounds 2-3
used, and not an arbitrary trivial family), asking how many of the 3 possible next-digit lifts
mod `3^{l+1}` actually occur per residue class.

**Ran the real test, using the already-verified DP extended one level up**
(`verify_round8_liftmask.py`):

```
l    j  units_mod   mask1  mask2  mask3   frac_mask3
2    4          6       1      1      4        0.667
3    6         18       1      1     16        0.889
4    7         54       3      3     48        0.889
5    9        162       0      3    159        0.981
6   10        486       1      7    478        0.984
7   11       1458       3     16   1439        0.987
8   12       4374       6     36   4332        0.990
```

**A genuinely new, positive result**: the fraction of residues with a full 3-way next-digit split
(`mask3`) climbs steadily toward 1 as `l` grows (66.7% -> 88.9% -> 88.9% -> 98.1% -> 98.4% ->
98.7% -> 99.0%), the opposite trend from every other local-construction quantity measured so far
in this cycle (rectangle-coverage fraction declined with `l`; the triple-packing `B_opt/U` grew
with `l`). A small number of "stuck" residues (`mask1`, zero next-digit flexibility) persist but
shrink as a fraction. Sent to Codex for round 9 (the last technical round; round 10 reserved for
final synthesis): whether this is load-bearing for GAP A/`j*(l)`'s rate or a real-but-tangential
structural fact, what's most valuable to check with one round left, and what the persistent
`mask1` residues might mean.

**Round 9: Codex gave a calibrated, honest verdict and a precise final test.** "This is the
strongest positive local signal so far. But by itself it does not close GAP A or say anything
rigorous about the `j*(l)` rate. It says the obstruction has moved from the bulk to a very small
exceptional set." Computed the actual missed-child count `|D_l| = 2*mask1+mask2` (`3, 3, 9, 3, 9,
22, 48` for `l=2..8`, `0.366%` of all children at `l=8`), independently checked (matches the
round-8 raw counts exactly by construction). Correctly flagged the real risk: "a single nested
3-adic bad branch can have density tending exponentially to zero while still forcing a new
obstruction at every level," so density alone doesn't prove anything. Proposed the decisive final
experiment instead of the naive "one persistent full-mask residue mod 9" idea (explicitly rejected
as "too easy to be decisive"): a **defect-transition/repair test** -- for every residue that was
"missing" (a defect) at level `l`'s real budget `j*(l)`, check its own mask at level `l+1`'s real,
bigger budget `j*(l+1)`, and see whether defects get repaired, persist as a recognizable type, or
propagate indefinitely (the three scenarios: best/danger/intermediate case, precisely
distinguished in advance).

**Ran the test ourselves immediately, using the already-verified DP, rather than waiting for round
10** (`verify_round9_transition.py`): for `l=2` through `l=7`, took every "missing" child mod
`3^{l+1}` (the defect set from round 8's masks, using budget `j*(l)`) and computed its own mask mod
`3^{l+2}` using the real, bigger budget `j*(l+1)`:

```
l=2->3: missing n=3,  new mask3=3/3  (100%)  | attained n=15,   new mask3=13/15   (86.7%)
l=3->4: missing n=3,  new mask3=3/3  (100%)  | attained n=51,   new mask3=45/51   (88.2%)
l=4->5: missing n=9,  new mask3=9/9  (100%)  | attained n=153,  new mask3=150/153 (98.0%)
l=5->6: missing n=3,  new mask3=3/3  (100%)  | attained n=483,  new mask3=475/483 (98.3%)
l=6->7: missing n=9,  new mask3=9/9  (100%)  | attained n=1449, new mask3=1430/1449(98.7%)
l=7->8: missing n=22, new mask3=22/22(100%)  | attained n=4352, new mask3=4310/4352(99.0%)
```

**Every single previously-missing child, across all six transitions (49 total defect instances),
gets a full 3-way mask at the next real budget, with zero exceptions.** This is exactly Codex's
"best case" scenario: old holes repair into the full-mask core, no evidence of a persistent defect
type recurring under the real budget transition, at every stratification checked (including a
full breakdown by old-parent-mask-size, same picture throughout). Sent to Codex for round 10 (the
final round), asking whether this changes its calibrated view on tractability given it explicitly
asked for exactly this upgrade from density to repair statement.

**Round 10 (final synthesis), 2026-08-01.** Codex's answers, recorded as its own stated view, not
independently re-derived by us this round:

1. **Most valuable outcome of the whole cycle**: "the lift-mask/defect-repair framework at the
   real budget... defects are not hereditary; observed defects are absorbed into full-flexibility
   states one level later. That is much closer to an exact-rate proof than the spectral
   identification or any of the abandoned constructions." The Young-diagram reformulation (round
   1) remains "the strongest durable reformulation," with the mask dynamics now "the leading live
   route."
2. **Calibrated confidence update, given explicitly**: moves a genuinely local/whole-tuple route
   to the exact `j*(l)` rate from "interesting but unlikely" (`<=20%`) to "the leading plausible
   mechanism" (`40-45%`), with an honest structural caveat attached: "new holes can still be born
   from attained parents with incomplete masks. Thus one-step repair alone does not imply eventual
   full coverage or the desired rate," and "49 repairs are not independent samples... 'climbing
   toward 1' is an observed finite-range trend, not an asymptotic conclusion." The remaining
   question reframed as "whether one can prove a finite-type, witness-compatible repair theorem,"
   not whether a local mechanism is compatible with the real budget at all (which the whole cycle
   settled, positively, for THIS mechanism specifically).
3. **Recommended next step**: prove a finite-state **defect genealogy lemma** at the true budget
   -- enrich the DP state beyond the bare mask with bounded carry/boundary/slack data sufficient
   to preserve actual witnesses, then prove a transition table where every partial-mask state
   either stays in the full core or produces only defect states that become full-mask after one
   real-budget step. Explicit methodological note: "Do not primarily extend the numerical range;
   use computation only to discover and minimize the candidate state types for that theorem" --
   i.e. the next work is proof construction guided by small-scale computation, not a bigger l-sweep.
4. **Self-flagged overreads, none requiring new retraction beyond rounds 5-6 (already handled)**:
   the window-2/transducer gadget's failure shows only that "*that gadget* is too expensive, not
   that local or whole-tuple arguments are too expensive" (i.e. round 9-10's positive result does
   not contradict rounds 2-4's negative one, they concern different mechanisms, consistent with
   our own round-8 pushback); the Macdonald identification "has not yet supplied coefficient or
   support control relevant to GAP A" and should not be treated as an active route without a new
   bridge; "99% full masks" and "100% repair" are "strong evidence, not a theorem," supporting "a
   sharply formulated repair conjecture" rather than establishing one.

**Overall assessment of this second 10-round cycle (rounds 1-10 of this consultation, recorded
here as H-003's rounds 31 as a whole)**: unlike the first 10-round cycle (recorded as round 30),
which ended with GAP A "exhausted" across every route tried, this cycle ends with GAP A's status
changed: the Fourier/character/transfer-operator family remains exhausted (confirmed again in
rounds 5-6, including catching a disguised repeat), but a genuinely new, independently verified,
positive empirical result now exists (the mask3-climbing and 100%-defect-repair data, rounds 8-9),
naming a concrete, well-defined remaining target (a finite-state defect-genealogy/repair theorem)
that neither this project nor Codex had previously formulated. Not a proof, and explicitly not
claimed as one by either side; a real, verified upgrade to the state of the open question, with
its own new backlog entry warranted (see H-011 in HYPOTHESES.md).

## Round 32 (2026-08-01): a fourth consultation cycle, steered away from everything closed across
## rounds 30-31 (the Fourier/character/transfer-operator family AND the local-lifting-mechanism
## family), asking again for genuinely lateral ideas, planned up to 10 rounds

Prompt listed both closed families explicitly (GAP A's Fourier/character/transfer-operator route,
and the window-gadget/transducer/triple-packing/q_l-shift local-lifting family), described the
Young-diagram/Macdonald identification and the mask/defect-repair data as available-but-not-to-
rederive substrate, and asked for a genuinely different angle.

**Round 1 response, independently verified before recording, per Rule 8c** (the codex exec call
itself needed two retries: the first was killed with no output by a stray earlier session process,
the second was killed by severe memory pressure from H-001's concurrently-running l=24 computation
saturating RAM and swap; l=24 was paused, per the researcher's explicit choice, to let this cycle
run without contention, to be relaunched once the cycle finishes):

Four concrete new algebraic facts proposed, independently checked:

1. **Mahler/digital-Young jet identity, CONFIRMED exact**: writing `alpha_i = eps_i + 2*n_i`
   (`eps_i` a parity bit), `F(alpha) = sum_i 3^i 2^eps_i 4^n_i = sum_r 3^r * sum_{i<=r} 2^eps_i *
   C(n_i, r-i)`, a binomial-theorem expansion of `4^n_i=(1+3)^n_i`. Verified against direct
   computation, 100 random trials, exact match.
2. **`v_3(4^(3^m)-1) = m+1`, CONFIRMED exact**, m=0..6 (a lifting-the-exponent consequence of
   `v_3(4-1)=1`).
3. **A noncommutative matrix representation, CONFIRMED exact**: `M_a := [[1,2^a],[0,3]]`,
   `M_{alpha_{j-1}} ... M_{alpha_0} * (0,1)^T = (F(alpha), 3^j)^T`. Verified, 100 random trials,
   j up to 8, exact match every time.
4. **An adjacent-rank-swap value-change formula, confirmed up to a labeling-convention sign**:
   claimed `diff = -2*3^i*(2^a-2^b)`; direct computation across 50 trials matched in magnitude
   exactly every time but with the opposite sign (`diff/claimed = -1.0` in all 50 cases, never
   anything else), i.e. the correct formula is `diff = +2*3^i*(2^a-2^b)` under our indexing
   convention. A trivial convention mismatch, not a substantive error.

Two structural proposals built on these confirmed identities, not yet independently tested:
a "digital Young diagram with carry avalanches" renormalization (using fact 2's precision-shift
rule to turn the rank-value coupling into a diagonal grading, with a conjectural "multiscale
barrier certificate" theorem format for missing residues); and a Durfee-square factorization
(`F_j(lambda) = 3^j-2^j+2^j*H_{j,j}(lambda)`, with `H_{a,b}` splitting across the Durfee square
into independent top-right/bottom-left codebook pieces, proposing a fiberwise zero-error
successive-refinement sufficient condition for coverage). Both are Codex's own top picks for
round 2, but the first computational test specified for the digital-Young route ("mod-3
leading-influence matrix... rank by output diagonal... recurring multiscale barrier profiles")
is not yet concrete enough to implement without guessing, and the Durfee `H_{a,b}` notation
never actually uses `b` in the given formula. Sent back to Codex for round 2: a fully concrete,
codeable specification of the digital-Young experiment, and clarification of the Durfee notation,
before spending compute on either.

**Round 2: Codex gave a fully precise, codeable specification, load-bearing lemma confirmed
exactly, but the actual experiment stalled on witness scarcity.** The key clarification: the
"Mahler leading diagonal" `i+3^m` (the first output row `r` where changing digit `m` of `n_i`
can possibly be felt) is a DIFFERENT, sharper quantity than the earlier "valuation depth"
`i+m+1` (a coarser sufficient-invariance bound); both real, not contradictory, operating at
different scales (`3^m` vs `m+1`). The core lemma:
`L_{r,(i,m)} = (1/2)*[J_r(eps,n+3^m e_i)-J_r(eps,n-3^m e_i)] (mod 3)` (with `1/2` the F_3
inverse of 2), claimed `L=0` for `r<i+3^m` and `L=2^eps_i` exactly at `r=i+3^m`.
**Independently verified, 2612 checks across 300 random configurations, all match** -- after
catching our own misreading (treating "1/2" as literal integer division rather than the F_3
inverse, which produced spurious "odd diff" failures on the first attempt; fixed once the error
was traced). Durfee `H_{a,b}` notation also clarified (`b` is a structural domain bound on the
sub-partition passed in, not an explicit truncation term).

**Attempted the actual buffered-cell/rank experiment on real witness tuples (brute-force,
exact), l=4 and l=6: zero buffered admissible cells found in every tested configuration**, even
at the smallest shift size (2). Flagged a likely confound rather than treating this as
conclusive: our witness-selection method always picks the lexicographically-first (densest,
most tightly-packed) member of each fiber, not a representative or random one, and real fibers
have hundreds to thousands of members at these budgets. Sent back to Codex for round 3: whether
this is a real structural fact (tuples are too densely packed on average for this no-carry
mechanism, a possible third "real math, wrong regime" result) or a witness-selection artifact
needing a different search strategy, and whether the buffered (two-sided) requirement should be
relaxed to a one-sided version.

**Round 3: Codex gave a real slack-bound explanation for the two-sided scarcity, plus a
one-sided relaxation with a stated caution -- and the caution turned out to be unnecessary.**
Slack bound: with `E := (a_j-a_1)-(j-1)` (total gap-slack beyond minimal packing) and `s=2*3^m`
the shift size, a buffered (two-sided) cell needs `E>=2s`; since the real budget forces `E<=j`,
`m>=1` (shift 6) is IMPOSSIBLE whenever `j<12`, matching both our tested scales exactly.
**Independently checked our own witnesses' actual `E`**: the l=4 near-defect witness had `E=2`
(`<4`, confirming impossibility exactly as predicted), but the other three witnesses had `E>=4`
(not ruled out by the aggregate bound) and STILL produced zero cells -- so the slack bound is a
real necessary condition but not sufficient; the specific digit-0-at-a-slack-position coincidence
does more work than aggregate slack alone.

Codex also proposed a one-sided relaxation (`D+`, `D-`, needing only one direction to stay
admissible, not both), but flagged real risk: "one-sided columns are not automatically
Lucas-triangular... the contaminant is the second-difference column `Q`... works only if `Q`
vanishes through the pivot diagonal. Do not infer this from the confirmed centered lemma."
**Tested directly, independently: 2218 checks across 300 random configurations, confirming both
the algebraic identity `L=2*(D++D-) (mod 3)` AND that `D+` and `D-` EACH independently have the
same clean Lucas property as the centered `L`, in every case.** `Q` does vanish through the pivot
diagonal; the caution turned out unnecessary, a genuine positive surprise. Recounted cells on the
same four witnesses using one-sided admissibility: nonzero everywhere now (1-4 cells at M<=0,
rising to 2-5 at M<=2), versus zero everywhere for the two-sided version, though still below `j`
(the count likely needed for a full-rank construction) in every tested case. Sent to Codex for
round 4: whether a partial-rank result has independent value, whether to push further in scale or
witness sampling, and whether to prioritize this one-sided route or the still-untested Durfee
factorization (Codex's own prior recommendation, on the grounds that Durfee "does not demand
local empty space the real tuples simply do not possess," though that specific advantage no
longer distinguishes the two routes now that the one-sided digital-Young route also survives
density scrutiny).

**Round 4: Codex withdrew its earlier caution outright** ("if D± has the same Lucas pivot
structure, it is a legitimate triangular-minor mechanism, not merely a heuristic variant"), gave
a precise target statistic (`nu_M(A)`, the count of distinct leading diagonals achieved, which
given the already-confirmed strict triangularity IS the maximum-rank/matching quantity, no
separate matching algorithm needed), a bound on the useful scale (`M_max` small, `M<=2` already
close to it for these `j`), and an explicit criterion for the round-5 pivot decision: "keep
Durfee as the main architecture unless [this] test reveals either `nu_{M_max}=j` systematically
or a stable recursive peeling rule."

**Ran the exact test specified, across whole fibers (not single witnesses)**
(`round4b_fiber_nu.py`): `nu_2` min/max across every fiber member, for the same four
near-defect/control residues used throughout this thread:

```
l=4 near-defect fiber: j=7,  fiber size=1,  nu_2 min=3 max=3  (need nu=7)
l=4 control fiber:     j=7,  fiber size=10, nu_2 min=2 max=4  (need nu=7)
l=6 near-defect fiber: j=10, fiber size=2,  nu_2 min=3 max=5  (need nu=10)
l=6 control fiber:     j=10, fiber size=5,  nu_2 min=2 max=2  (need nu=10)
```

**Even with full-fiber search, `nu_max` never approaches `j`** (best case 5 of 10). By Codex's
own stated criterion, this does not license continuing the one-sided digital-Young route as the
primary mechanism. Recorded as a fourth "real math, wrong regime" result in this project's
running count (real, cleanly verified Lucas structure; genuine partial-rank minor; falls well
short of full rank; no sign of closing the gap with more scale or more witnesses at this size).
Sent to Codex for round 5, pivoting to the still-untested Durfee-square factorization as the new
primary route, per Codex's own prior recommendation.

**Round 5: Codex agreed with the pivot cleanly, then proposed an elaborate 3x3-core
Schur-complement peeling architecture built on a claimed recurrence that turned out to be
false.** Codex cited "the Round-2 identity `j(l)=j(l-2)+3`" (no such identity was ever
established in round 2 of this cycle) and picked `l=8` as the "decision-scale test" specifically
because "`j(8)=13=3+j(6)`".

**Checked directly against the real table before writing any implementation code, per Rule 8c,
and it is false**: `j(8)=12`, not `13`; `j(8)-j(6)=2`, not `3`. Computing the full
`j(l)-j(l-2)` sequence for `l=3..23` (`5,3,3,3,2,2,2,3,3,2,2,2,2,1,1,2,2,2,2,2,2`) shows it is
neither constant nor simply periodic; the one run of three consecutive "+3" steps (`l=4,5,6`)
happens to be exactly the window Codex checked by hand, and `l=8` (chosen as the decisive test)
is actually a "+2" step. This is not a minor slip: it is the load-bearing premise for the entire
proposed 3x3-core-per-2-levels architecture, and with the real irregular difference sequence, a
fixed-size-3 core cannot work uniformly the way the proposal needs. **Not implemented** -- sent
back to Codex for round 6 asking either for a corrected version using the real, level-dependent
core sizes, or a return to the ORIGINAL round-1 Durfee proposal (the codebook-saturation
sufficient condition, which never depended on any `j(l)` recurrence and remains completely
untested).

**Round 6: Codex accepted the correction cleanly** ("You're correct... The fixed 3x3 peeling
proposal has no valid foundation and should be dropped, not patched") and returned to the
original recurrence-free Durfee codebook-saturation route, specifying a first test at real `l=3..8`.

**Filled a real gap Codex never actually specified across rounds 1-6: `C_d`'s formula.** Round 1
introduced `H_{j,j}(lambda) = C_d + 2^d*H_{d,j-d}(A) + (3/2)^d*H_{j-d,d}(B)` without ever defining
`C_d`; round 2's notation clarification covered only the `H_{a,b}` domain bound, not this
constant. **Derived it by hand** from the already-confirmed `H_{a,b}` formula, splitting the
Young diagram row-by-row through the Durfee square: `C_d = (2^d-1)*((3/2)^d-1)` (the `d x d`
square's own contribution). **Verified the entire decomposition, including this derived `C_d`,
against direct `F_j` computation in exact rational arithmetic: 200 random trials, exact match
every time** (`round6_durfee.py`).

**Ran the actual codebook-saturation test at real `(l,j)`, `d=1..j-1`**
(`round6b_codebook_saturation.py`; results for `d>=l` are a degenerate edge case, `l-d<=0`,
discarded as meaningless):

```
l=3,j=6:  d=1: 0/2   | d=2: 5/6 (83%)
l=4,j=7:  d=1: 0/2   | d=2: 3/6 (50%)  | d=3: 11/15 (73%)
l=5,j=9:  d=1: 0/2   | d=2: 0/6        | d=3: 16/18 (89%) | d=4: 34/44 (77%)
l=6,j=10: d=1: 0/2   | d=2: 0/6        | d=3: 13/18 (72%) | d=4: 44/51 (86%) | d=5: 69/104 (66%)
```

**No single `d` gives full saturation (the sufficient condition, as literally stated, fails at
every tested `d`), but `d` close to `l` gets remarkably close (66-89%)** -- a genuinely different
shape from every earlier route this cycle (not near-total failure like the digital-Young routes,
not exact success either). Sent to Codex for round 7: whether combining multiple Durfee strata
(different `d`) could cover what any single `d` misses, whether the un-saturated `u`'s have a
recognizable pattern, and whether 66-89% suggests a nearby true-but-harder statement or the wrong
container entirely.

**Round 7: Codex gave a real correction to how we'd been reading the numbers, and specified the
right multi-stratum test.** The round-6 percentages ("83% saturated" etc.) were conditional
fiber-saturation rates among ACHIEVED `u` values, not the actual fraction of `Z/3^l` covered
(round-6's `d=2,l=3` case: 5/6 achieved `u`'s fully saturate, but only 6 of the 9 possible `u`
classes were even achieved, so the real covered fraction is at most `6/9`, not `83%`). Specified
the correct test: compute the actual stratum image `S_d` (converting `(u,high)` pairs back to
genuine residues mod `3^l` via `T_d`, including `C_d` and the unit powers of 2), its complement
`E_d`, and the overlap structure `|E_d ∩ E_e|` across multiple `d`, to see whether a union of a
few strata's images covers everything.

**Ran the exact test specified, l=6, j=10, d=3,4,5** (`round7_stratum_union.py`):

```
d=3: |S_3 ∩ units| = 478/486 (98.4%), |E_3|=8
d=4: |S_4 ∩ units| = 438/486 (90.1%), |E_4|=48
d=5: |S_5 ∩ units| = 277/486 (57.0%), |E_5|=209

|E_3 ∩ E_4| = 2   |E_3 ∩ E_5| = 2   |E_4 ∩ E_5| = 48   |E_3 ∩ E_4 ∩ E_5| = 2
|S_3 ∪ S_4 ∪ S_5| = 484/486 units (99.59%), missing only {262, 505} mod 729
```

**A real, decisive, and much stronger result than round 6's raw numbers suggested**: three
Durfee strata alone (out of roughly `j=10` possible `d` values) already cover 99.59% of units,
leaving only 2 residues uncovered. Since coverage at `j*(6)=10` is already known (this Durfee
decomposition is a reparametrization of the same object confirmed in round 1), the interesting
content is how FEW, how structured a set of strata suffices, and whether this could ever yield a
clean non-computational covering argument rather than relying on all `~j` strata implicitly (as
the brute-force covering DP does). Sent to Codex for round 8: what distinguishes the two holdout
residues (`505-262=243=3^5`, possibly meaningful), whether adjacent strata (`d=2` or `d=6`) close
the gap, and whether this near-complete-from-few-strata pattern should be expected to hold or
degrade as `l` grows.

**Round 8: Codex's 3-adic explanation of the two holdouts checked out exactly, then testing its
own suggested adjacent stratum turned near-complete into completely proven, and the pattern
generalizes.** Codex derived: `262 = 100201_3`, `505 = 200201_3` (share their five low base-3
digits), both `≡ 19 (mod 3^5)`, and are exactly the two nonzero multiplicative lifts
`19*(1+3^5)` and `19*(1+2*3^5) (mod 3^6)` of the same residue `19` (whose zero lift, `19` itself,
is already covered). **Independently verified all of this arithmetic exactly** (base-3
representations, the mod-`3^5` congruence, and both lift formulas, `19*244=4636≡262` and
`19*487=9253≡505 (mod 729)`).

**Then tested `d=2` directly (one of the two adjacent strata Codex asked about): it covers BOTH
holdouts.** `S_2 ∪ S_3` alone gets to 483/486 (3 different residues left over: 37, 136, 307), but
**`S_2 ∪ S_3 ∪ S_4` = 486/486, full coverage** (`round8_final_union.py`). **Checked whether "a
small number of low-`d` strata suffices" generalizes beyond `l=6` (`round8_scaling_check.py`),
greedily adding strata `d=1,2,3,...` until the union is complete**:

```
l=4, j=7:  FULL COVERAGE using d={1,2,3}       (3 of up to 3 possible)
l=5, j=9:  FULL COVERAGE using d={1,2,3}       (3 of up to 4 possible)
l=6, j=10: FULL COVERAGE using d up to 4       (confirmed above)
l=7, j=11: FULL COVERAGE using d={1,2,3,4,5}   (5 of up to 6 possible)
```

**A real, decisive, positive multi-stratum covering result, confirmed at every one of four
tested `l` values, using only strata up to roughly `d~l-2`, well short of the full range to
`d~j`.** This is the strongest positive finding of the whole 10-round cycle. Sent to Codex for
round 9 (second-to-last technical round): whether the "low `d` suffices" pattern has a real
derivation (not just 4 data points), whether it could give a structurally new bound on `j*(l)`,
and what's most valuable to check with the rounds remaining.

**Round 9: Codex gave a precise, honest correction rather than accepting the framing at face
value.** Called the multi-stratum result "a real breakthrough for the Durfee approach" but flatly
correct that it does not yet prove a low-`d` theorem: "the observed maximum needed `d` is
`3,3,4,5` for `l=4,5,6,7`. That supports `d<=l+O(1)`, but not `d<=c*l` for any fixed `c<1`:
`l-2` has asymptotic coefficient 1." Also correctly separated "number of strata used" from
"`j*(l)`'s slope": only a repeatable, cost-bounded LIFTING GADGET (a rule taking a covering
witness at level `l` to one at `l+1` with bounded `Delta j`, `Delta d`) would translate into a
new rate bound, e.g. `Delta j=1` per level would give `j*(l)<=l+O(1)`, genuinely stronger than
the existing `119/104*l` mean-payoff-game bound; the raw stratum-count data alone says nothing
about this. Proposed the real final-round target: derive or falsify a witness-level
fiber-lifting rule from `(l,j)=(6,10)` to `(7,11)`, using the `19 mod 3^5` example as the
concrete local phenomenon to generalize. **Not attempted**: this is a substantial investigation
on its own, and with only round 10 (reserved for final synthesis, matching this project's
established practice) left, doing it properly was judged out of scope for this cycle rather than
rushing a half-verified attempt.

**Round 10 (final synthesis), 2026-08-01.** Codex's answers, recorded as its own stated view:

1. **Most valuable outcome of the whole cycle**: "the Durfee multi-stratum compression
   phenomenon, together with the exact decomposition that makes it testable... the first result
   in this cycle that exposes a plausible route from a finite covering computation to an
   inductive construction."
2. **Calibrated read on the Durfee result, given explicitly**: "genuinely structural and worth
   pursuing, but not yet evidence for a rate bound... too organized to dismiss as decorative
   numerology" but "the missing statement is exactly the decisive one: a uniform, witness-level,
   bounded-cost lifting rule. Without it, 'low relative to `j`' is only a static fact at several
   small levels... gives no slope information for `j*(l)`." Labeled it **"a live structural
   program, medium promise / high uncertainty -- stronger than an interesting observation, weaker
   than a credible rate proof strategy until the lifting lemma survives direct testing."**
3. **Recommended next step**: the witness-level lifting-rule investigation from round 9,
   starting with the complete `(l,j)=(6,10) -> (7,11)` child-fiber check under `Delta d<=1`; a
   positive result would promote the Durfee route to an inductive candidate, a negative result
   with classified obstructions would be equally informative. Explicit ranking against the
   PREVIOUS cycle's mask/defect-repair thread (H-011): "I would keep [it] as the stronger
   alternative only if it already has a comparably concrete, repeatable local rule with bounded
   cost. On the information here, the Durfee lifting test comes first because it directly tests
   the newly identified bottleneck."
4. **Self-flagged overstatement risks, none requiring new retraction beyond rounds 5-6 and
   round 2-4 (already handled)**: treating `d~l-2` as a constant-fraction saving (it is not,
   asymptotic coefficient 1); treating four consecutive exact small cases as evidence of an
   induction ("they establish a phenomenon, not its persistence"); over-crediting the single
   `19 mod 3^5` repair example ("proves feasibility for one witness/child situation, not a repair
   rule for arbitrary missing children"); calling the Mahler/LTE/matrix/Lucas results "progress
   toward GAP A" without the qualification that the digital-Young rank mechanism itself is
   confirmed to be in the wrong regime for the main target.

**Overall assessment of this fourth consultation cycle (round 32 as a whole, second full
10-round lateral cycle this session)**: like round 31, ends without a proof, but with a real,
independently verified new structural object (the complete Durfee decomposition, including the
previously-unspecified `C_d`) and a genuinely positive, if uncertain, empirical phenomenon (small
multi-stratum unions achieving full coverage at every tested `l`), plus four more closed dead
ends recorded honestly (one-sided digital-Young's rank shortfall, a caught fabricated recurrence,
and the earlier GAP A/S(t) repeat from rounds 5-6). The single most concrete open task this cycle
produced -- the witness-level Durfee lifting-rule investigation -- is tracked as a new backlog
hypothesis (see H-012 in HYPOTHESES.md), the same treatment given to the mask/defect-repair
thread (H-011) after the previous cycle.

## Round 33 (2026-08-02): a fifth consultation cycle, planned for up to 50 rounds, deliberately
## open-ended rather than steered toward any single thread -- the full closed-route catalog and
## both open leads (H-011, H-012) given up front, explicitly inviting genuinely lateral ideas
## from any field, with a request for volume (many candidate directions) over depth in round 1

Prompt gave the complete state of the project (every closed route across rounds 30-32, both
survived objects/open leads H-011 and H-012) and asked for as many genuinely distinct angles as
possible in one response, not fixated on any single thread, explicitly inviting cross-disciplinary
and newly-invented-formalism proposals. `l=24` (H-001) paused via `SIGSTOP` for the duration
(researcher's explicit choice, fully reversible, no progress at risk) to give this cycle the
machine's full memory.

**Round 1 response: fifteen distinct candidate directions, plus one "certain confidence" claim
about the problem's own structure, independently checked before recording anything.**

**The "certain confidence" claim, split into what's new and what isn't**: for `j>=l`, only
`alpha_0..alpha_{l-1}` affect `F_j(alpha) mod 3^l` -- this part is NOT new, it is foundational to
the whole project (the `R_{l-1,l}` naming itself depends on it). The genuinely new part: a valid
prefix `alpha_0>...>alpha_{l-1}>=0` extends to a full admissible `j`-term tuple **iff
`alpha_{l-1} >= j-l`**. **Independently verified, 500 random trials, brute-force cross-check, no
mismatches** (`mega_round1_verify2.py`). This reframes H-012's earlier "`d<=l-2` strata suffice"
finding: since the effective maximum Durfee size is capped at `l` (not `j`) once `j>=l`, the
result is "all but the top ~2 of `l` EFFECTIVE strata," a cleaner and stronger-sounding
restatement of the same underlying fact, not new data. Also confirmed the box-weight carry
identity `w_{i,c}+w_{i,c+1}=w_{i+1,c+1}` (trivial by hand, `1+2=3` embedded diagonally in the
weight formula) underlying the proposed "carry-tile" idea.

**Fourteen more speculative directions proposed (rectangular-cut recursion generalizing Durfee,
Ferrers carry-tile renormalization, an RG coarse-graining of carry tiles, ball-saturation of
strata, ultrametric exponent clustering via `v_3(2^a-2^b)`, a controlled-3-adic-dynamics/online-
synthesis framing, a cyclic exponent-subset rotation action, integral Macdonald theory at the
resonant ratio `q/t=3`, Witt-vector coordinates, tropical target-avoidance geometry, poset-toggle/
discrete-Morse search, symbolic-dynamics cocycle essential values, a coding-theoretic
redundancy framing, replica/spin-glass heuristics for discovery only, and a Bruhat-Tits
viability-kernel framing), none yet tested.** Given fifteen ideas is far more than can be given
real rigor per round across even a 50-round cycle, sent back to Codex for round 2 asking it to
rank its own top 3-4 and specify the smallest concrete first test for each, rather than this
project shallow-testing all fifteen; also flagged that item 13 (coding-theoretic redundancy)
looks like a restatement of the already-known pigeonhole/counting picture (`M_{j,l}` estimates
from round 31's round 1) rather than new content, asking Codex to confirm or correct that read.
**Round 2: Codex prioritized its own list, agreed with the item-13 dismissal, and adopted the
reframing.** Ranked order: (1) rectangular-cut recursion (generalizing Durfee), (2) carry-tile
global connectivity, (3) a boundary-state classification behind H-012 (`d<=l-2` reframed as
"all but the top ~2 of `l` effective strata"), (4) a conditional transfer rule, only if (1) or
(3) produces a compact state table. Confirmed the reframing: "via exact extendability, the
`d<=l-2` result controls all but the top roughly two of the `l` effective strata." Confirmed item
13 is not new content, as suspected.

**Ran round 2's top-priority test: the generalized rectangular-cut formula.** Independently
re-derived it by hand first (a direct generalization of the already-verified Durfee derivation,
splitting rows 1..h vs h+1..l and, within rows 1..h, columns 1..m vs m+1..lambda_r), matching
Codex's formula exactly, including `C_{h,m}=(2^m-1)*((3/2)^h-1)`. **Verified computationally: 300
random trials, using partitions genuinely constructed to respect the (h,m) cut (an earlier version
of this test forced an arbitrary cut onto a random partition and was wrong for exactly that
reason, caught before trusting it) -- all match exactly.**

**A real, decisive new finding from testing the generalization against the known l=6 holdouts
{262, 505}**: swept off-diagonal `(h,m)` strata (`h != m`) and found several that cover both
holdouts, most notably `h=1, m=2` giving a stratum of size only 132 that covers BOTH residues on
its own -- markedly smaller than any of the diagonal strata (`d=3,4,5`, sizes 209-478) that
together still needed a fourth diagonal stratum to close the gap. `h=5,m=0` and `h=6,m=0`
trivially cover everything (close to the unrestricted full construction, flagged as not a real
compression). Sent to Codex for round 3: whether `h=1` (single-row cuts) has a structural reason
for this efficiency, whether a genuinely smaller minimal covering certificate exists via
off-diagonal cuts, and whether there's a clean characterization of which residues an `(h,m)`
stratum covers.

**Round 3: Codex derived a collapsed closed form for h=1 specifically, and predicted a testable
"same-row" signature.** For `h=1`, writing `r=lambda_1` and describing the tail `B` by its
conjugate column heights `c_1>=...>=c_m>=0`:

```
H(lambda) = 2^{r-1} - 1/2 + (3/2) * sum_{q=1}^m 2^{q-1} * [(3/2)^{c_q} - 1]
```

Independently re-derived this by hand (after catching and fixing our own row-index slip,
`c-r-1` not `c-1`, mid-derivation), matching exactly. **Verified computationally: 200 random
trials (random `l,j,m,r`, tail heights reconstructed into an actual partition, compared against
the full `H_{l,j}` sum), exact match every time.** Confirmed the parameter-count claims too by
hand (`h=1,m=2` at `(6,10)` has `9*21=189` parameter tuples vs `675` for the unconstrained
diagonal `d=2`, both checked). Codex's diagnostic prediction: if the two `l=6` holdouts share the
same top-row value `r` in their `(r,x,y)` witnesses, that is "strong evidence of the clean
recursive mechanism" (a tail-lift, not competing translates).

**Searched the full h=1,m=2 parameter space for witnesses: residue 262 -> (r,x,y)=(7,4,3),
residue 505 -> (r,x,y)=(7,2,2). Same r=7 for both** -- exactly Codex's predicted "clean
mechanism" signature, not the alternative. Sent to Codex for round 4: agreed to continue the
rectangular-cut thread per its own "short decisive sprint" plan, asked for a specific falsifiable
prediction to test before scanning `h=1, m=1..4` across `l=4..8` (rather than just collecting
more examples).

**Round 4: Codex pre-registered a complete, general falsifiable law, confirmed exactly.** With
`U(x,y) := (3/2)^x-1 + 2*((3/2)^y-1)`, claimed `U(a+2,a+1)-U(a,a) = (3/2)^{a+2}`, and for
`P_l=(l-2,l-3)`, `Q_l=(l-4,l-4)`: `H(r,P_l)-H(r,Q_l) = (-1)^{l-1}*3^{l-1} (mod 3^l)` at ANY common
`r`, with an explicit prediction table (`+3^4,-3^5,+3^6,-3^7` for `l=5,6,7,8`). **Independently
verified both parts exactly**: the `U`-identity for `a=1..7`, and the full `H`-difference law at
`l=5,6,7,8`, each checked at four different `r` values (3,5,7,10) -- every single case matched
(`mega_round4_predict.py`). A real, clean, general algebraic law, not an `l=6` coincidence.

**Follow-up (same round, run before sending): does this exact pair actually repair the real
holdout residues at l=7,8 the way it did at l=6? Mixed result, honestly reported.** Using a
greedy increasing-`d` sweep, found the "last stratum needed" holdouts at l=7 (`d=5` needed for
residue `2002` alone) and l=8 (`d=5` needed for 5 residues). **The prescribed `(P_l,Q_l)` pair
does NOT hit any of these residues at either level.** Flagged a real methodological mismatch
before treating this as a refutation: the l=6 holdouts came from a different experimental setup
(testing high strata `d=3,4,5` first, discovering low stratum `d=2` repairs the gap afterward),
not a plain greedy increasing sweep; the l=7/8 tests here used the greedy sweep instead, so "last
stratum needed" isn't a like-for-like comparison. Sent to Codex for round 6: whether to redo
l=7/8 with the same high-strata-first methodology for a fair comparison, or whether this points
to the lift law being real structure without yet being tied to an actual repair mechanism ("real
math, right regime, not yet a proof-shaped claim"); also still owed a precise falsifiable claim
for the m=1 negative control.

**Round 5/6: Codex specified the fair, apples-to-apples test and a precise m=1 control; both
run, decisive results.** Fair test: `R_l = units \ (S_{l,3} u S_{l,4} u S_{l,5})` (the exact l=6
setup) at l=6,7,8. **l=6 reproduces the original finding exactly**: `R_6={262,505}`, both hit by
the `(P_l,Q_l)` pair, and the `tau_6`-pairing confirmed (`262 - tau_6 = 505` exactly,
`tau_6=-3^5`). **At l=7 and l=8, using this SAME methodology, genuinely different holdouts
appear** (`R_7={490,805,1750}`, `R_8={5650}`, distinct from the earlier greedy-method holdouts,
confirming the two methodologies really do ask different questions) **and the prescribed pair
hits NONE of them at either level.** Real, decisive: the l=6 "clean tail-lift repair" does not
generalize, even under a fair comparison.

**The m=1 negative control was falsified as literally stated, but with a real, honest nuance.**
Exhaustive check over every admissible `r`, `l=5..8`: the claimed difference is never zero, but
it IS constant across every `r` (`216,324,486,729` for `l=5..8`, each step exactly `x1.5`), with
3-adic valuation exactly `l-2` in every case -- one power of 3 less deep than the m=2 case's
`l-1`. Qualitatively consistent with Codex's broader claim (m=1 genuinely cannot reach the
`3^{l-1}`-scale cancellation m=2 achieves), even though the literal "exactly zero" prediction
was wrong. Recorded as partially correct, not a clean pass or fail.

**Closing this sub-thread as a repair-mechanism candidate**: real, multiply-confirmed algebra
(the general rectangular-cut formula, the collapsed h=1 form, the pre-registered lift law, and
the m=1-vs-m=2 valuation-depth relationship) that does not generalize into a repair theorem past
l=6. Moving to Codex's own round-2 priority #2 (carry-tile global connectivity) next round.

**Round 6 confirmed the pivot** ("Yes -- move to priority #2... Another round there would need a
genuinely new mechanism, not another variant of the same pair") and gave one wording correction
worth keeping: "m=1 does exhibit cancellation of valuation l-2; it fails to reach the m=2 depth
l-1" (not "m=1 cancels to zero," which was the literal but wrong original phrasing).

**Attempting the carry-tile connectivity test (round 2's priority #2) surfaced a real
formalization obstruction before any code was written.** Tried to formalize "legal carry-tile
move" from the box-weight identity `w_{i,c}+w_{i,c+1}=w_{i+1,c+1}` as removing two boxes from row
`i` and adding one to row `i+1`; found this generically produces an INVALID partition
(`lambda'_i < lambda'_{i+1}`, violating weakly-decreasing order) unless further constrained --
exactly the "order-ideal constraint is the key obstruction" issue flagged (but not resolved) in
round 1. Sent back to Codex for round 7 asking for the precise, fully-specified definition (single
move vs a compound bundle of simultaneous edits) plus one fully worked example, rather than
guessing and testing the wrong graph.

**Round 7: Codex re-derived its own round-1 proposal properly and found it has NO nontrivial
moves at all -- a real, self-corrected negative result.** Showed by direct case analysis that a
single carry move ALWAYS produces `lambda'_i < lambda'_{i+1}` (never weakly decreasing), and by
an inductive argument (examining the last move in any finite chain) that no finite compound of
carries can repair this either, the defect always propagating to row 1 unresolved. Gave a worked
example: `(5,4,4,4,0)` cascading through `(3,5,4,4,0) -> (3,3,5,4,0) -> (3,3,3,5,0) ->
(3,3,3,3,5)`. **Independently verified**: the carry identity itself for sampled `(i,c)`, and
mechanically checked weakly-decreasing order at every step of the worked example -- confirmed

(False,False): 374 covered, 24 NOT covered  <-- MIXED

The `(False,False)` bucket contains both statuses, directly falsifying the compression claim, one

**Round 9: Codex gave a fully precise statistical specification (ball-saturation depth, cluster
profiles, an exact combinatorial null via a tree-recursive polynomial DP), noting H-012 stays

v_3(x-y)` (ultrametric pair-clustering) and `K(R) = sum_q (162/b_q)*h(q,0)` (multiscale
saturated-ball score), with exact closed-form null means for a uniform-random 24-subset, and a
full recipe for the EXACT null distribution via a tree-recursive polynomial DP (cube the child
polynomial per level, shift the y-exponent by `C(m,2)` after each cubing, final squaring at the
root). Decision rule: `p_T<=0.01` and `p_K<=0.01` for a positive signal.

**Computed the observed statistics directly first**: `T(R_4)=569` vs mean `203.728` (ratio
2.79x); `K(R_4)=506` vs mean `282.9` (ratio 1.79x) (`mega_round9_saturation.py`). **Then built
the exact polynomial DP for T's full null distribution** (`mega_round9_exact_pvalue.py`), with
two strong internal sanity checks both passing: the DP's total count at `x^24` matches

exactly. **Result: `p_T = 6.697e-15`, a z-score of 24.7 -- roughly 15 orders of magnitude below
the 0.01 significance threshold.** The 24 residues the low-stratum construction misses at
`(6,8)` are 3-adically clustered far beyond anything a random 24-subset would produce; this looks
like the strongest single finding of the cycle so far. `p_K`'s exact DP not yet built (only the
1.79x mean-ratio signal so far); sent to Codex for round 10 to ask whether `T` alone is already
decisive, and what the right next step is to turn "the misses are clustered" into something

**Round 10: Codex confirmed `T` is decisive on its own** (flagging a look-elsewhere caution that

**Built the trie certificate (`mega_round10_trie.py`): a striking, concrete, fully verified
result.** Depth-by-depth occupancy of `R_4`'s 24 points, with collision increments summing
exactly to `T=569` (`213+213+100+34+9=569`, an internal consistency check). **At depth 2 (mod
9), all 24 points fall into just 2 of the 6 possible unit residue classes: 21 points at residue
4, 3 points at residue 8, zero elsewhere.** Confirmed directly via base-3 digit inspection: every
element's last two ternary digits are either `(1,1)` or `(2,2)`, never any other combination.
This is exactly the kind of "explicitly describable 3-adic cylinder" statement Codex asked for:
`R_4 subset {x=4 mod 9} u {x=8 mod 9}`. Sent to Codex for round 11: the precise next computation
to trace WHY the low-stratum construction (`d<=4`) fails specifically on the `{4,8} mod 9` class
and not the other four unit classes mod 9.

**Round 11: Codex specified the exact per-class breakdown to distinguish two hypotheses (a
common mod-9 obstruction in individual strata vs. a joint failure where otherwise-strong strata's
holes happen to align).** Ran it (`mega_round11_mod9_table.py`):

```
r  |U_r| |I1| |I2| |I3| |I4|  union  |R4∩U_r|
1    81   10   39   70   54    81       0
2    81    6   47   77   69    81       0
4    81    6   32   31    6    60      21
5    81   11   49   80   72    81       0
7    81    7   44   75   45    81       0
8    81    6   37   56   21    78       3
```

**A real, decisive answer: classes 1,2,5,7 reach FULL coverage (81/81) via strata `d<=4`; only
classes 4 and 8 fall short (60/81, 78/81), exactly where `R_4` lives.** Individual strata `I3` and
`I4` are dramatically, disproportionately weaker on class 4 (`31/81~38%`, `6/81~7%`, versus
`70-99%` and `56-89%` respectively on every other class) -- a real per-stratum weakness, not
coincidental hole-alignment across otherwise-strong strata. Class 8 shows the same pattern mainly
in `I4` (`21/81~26%` vs `45-72` elsewhere). Also computed the cumulative-residual shrinkage within
`{4,8} mod 9}` as strata are added (`162 -> 150 -> 86 -> 26 -> 24` for `t=0..4`, consistent with
the final count). Sent to Codex for round 12: confirm the pointwise-inversion next step (classify
